ABOUT THIS REPORT
REPORTING SCOPE AND BOUNDARY
The Integrated Annual Report for FY 2025-26 presents 
a comprehensive and cohesive account of Godawari 
Power & Ispat Limited’s (hereinafter referred to as 
“GPIL ”, “Company”, “we”, “our” or “us”) journey of creating 
sustainable value for its stakeholders. The report 
outlines how the Company leverages its integrated 
steel, iron ore mining and power business model, 
operational excellence, strategic investments and 
sustainability-led initiatives to deliver long-term value 
for shareholders, customers, employees, communities, 
regulators and business partners. This commitment 
underpins our value creation philosophy and reflects 
our focus on achieving sustainable growth while 
maintaining responsible business practices
FORWARD-LOOKING STATEMENTS
This report contains forward-looking statements 
regarding the expected future performance, business 
prospects, financial position and growth plans 
of the Company. These statements are based on 
management’s current expectations, assumptions 
and estimates and are subject to various risks and 
uncertainties. Actual results may differ materially 
from those expressed or implied due to changes in 
economic conditions, industry dynamics, regulatory 
developments and other factors beyond the Company’s 
control. Readers are advised not to place undue 
reliance on such statements. The Company undertakes 
no obligation to publicly update or revise any forward-
looking statements except as required by applicable 
laws and regulations. REPORTING PERIOD
This Integrated Annual Report covers the financial 
year from 1 April 2025 to 31 March 2026. The 
report includes material developments, operational 
performance, sustainability initiatives and significant 
events occurring during the reporting period and up 
to the date of approval of the report by the Board of 
Directors. The reporting boundary covers Godawari 
Power & Ispat Limited and its material subsidiaries and 
business operations.REPORTING PRINCIPLES 
AND FRAMEWORK
This Integrated Annual Report has been prepared 
in accordance with the following frameworks and 
regulatory requirements:
Companies Act, 2013 (including applicable 
 rules thereunder) 
• Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) 
 Rules, 2015 
• SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
 Requirements) Regulations, 2015 
• SEBI Business Responsibility and Sustainability 
 Reporting (BRSR) requirements 
• International Integrated Reporting Framework 
 (IIRC Framework) 
• Global Reporting Initiative (GRI) StandardsREPORTING SCOPE 
 AND BOUNDARY

THEME INTRO
CHARTING THE 
NEXT HORIZON
Every horizon reached reveals another waiting beyond it. Growth, therefore, is not 
a destination—it is a continuous pursuit of possibility. For Godawari Power & Ispat 
Limited, FY 2025-26 was a year of building momentum, sharpening capabilities 
and preparing the enterprise for a future defined by greater ambition and wider 
opportunities.
Anchored by an integrated business model and guided by a long-term vision, the 
Company focused on strengthening the engines that power progress. Each initiative, 
each improvement and each investment contributed to a larger objective: creating 
a stronger, more agile and future-ready organisation capable of unlocking the next 
phase of value creation.BUILDING MOMENTUM ACROSS THE VALUE CHAIN
Progress is often measured in numbers, but its true significance lies in what those numbers 
represent. Across operations, GPIL continued to demonstrate the strength of its integrated 
ecosystem, the discipline of execution and the resilience that has become a hallmark of 
its journey.
From mines to metals, from processing to value-added products, every link in the value 
chain contributed to a year marked by consistency, reliability and operational excellence. 
The collective performance reflected not only the efficiency of assets but also the power 
of integration, collaboration and a shared commitment to excellence.
These achievements reinforce a simple truth: sustainable growth is built when every part 
of the enterprise moves forward with purpose and alignment.
CREATING THE FOUNDATION FOR TOMORROW
The future belongs to organisations that prepare for it before it arrives. With this belief, 
GPIL continued to strengthen the foundations that will support its next chapter of growth.
The year witnessed meaningful progress across expansion initiatives, capability 
enhancement programmes and strategic approvals that broaden the Company’s growth 
runway. These efforts are not merely about adding scale; they are about creating greater 
flexibility, deeper integration and stronger readiness for emerging opportunities.
Every enhancement undertaken today is designed to unlock possibilities for tomorrow—
enabling GPIL to respond faster, operate smarter and compete more effectively in an 
evolving business landscape.
IGNITING SUSTAINABLE PROGRESS
The next horizon of growth demands more than expansion—it requires responsibility. 
As industries transform and sustainability becomes central to long-term competitiveness, 
GPIL continues to align growth with stewardship.
The Company remains focused on improving resource efficiency, embracing cleaner 
technologies and strengthening initiatives that support environmental responsibility. 
Investments in energy optimisation, process innovation, resource conservation and 
operational excellence reflect a broader commitment to building a business that is 
both resilient and sustainable.
For GPIL, sustainability is not a parallel agenda; it is an integral part of how future 
growth is envisioned and achieved.
LOOKING AHEAD
The horizon ahead is filled with promise. It represents new opportunities to create value, 
strengthen competitiveness and redefine what is possible.
As GPIL enters its next phase, the focus remains clear: to transform capability into 
opportunity, momentum into scale and ambition into lasting impact. The journey ahead 
will be guided by innovation, operational excellence, sustainability and an unwavering 
commitment to stakeholder value.
With a strong foundation, a clear strategic direction and the confidence that comes from 
purposeful execution, Godawari Power & Ispat Limited is ready to ignite the next phase of 
growth—advancing towards new horizons with energy, conviction and purpose.

ABOUT US
WHERE INTEGRATED STRENGTH 
MEETS FUTURE OPPORTUNITY
With an experience of more than 26 years, Godawari Power & Ispat Limited has 
emerged as one of India’s renowned integrated metals and energy companies, 
creating value through a diversified portfolio spanning natural resources, 
manufacturing and power generation. Over the years, the Company has transformed 
itself from a sponge iron producer into a strategically integrated enterprise with a 
strong presence across mining, iron ore beneficiation, pelletisation, steelmaking, 
ferro alloys and  power generation.
Anchored by a culture of operational excellence and 
prudent capital allocation, GPIL has consistently 
strengthened its capabilities, expanded its footprint 
and enhanced stakeholder value, establishing a strong 
reputation for efficiency, reliability and sustainable 
growth.
Beyond its operational capabilities, GPIL is guided by  
a commitment to responsible and sustainable growth.  
The Company continues to strengthen its business 
through disciplined capital allocation, operational 
excellence, technological advancement and a strong 
focus on environmental stewardship. Investments in 
renewable energy, resource efficiency, decarbonisation 
initiatives and circular economy practices reflect its 
efforts to build a future-ready enterprise. Equally 
important is GPIL ’s focus on its people, fostering a 
safe, inclusive and performance-driven workplace that 
encourages continuous learning and development. 
Supported by robust governance practices, stakeholder-
centric decision-making and a culture rooted in integrity 
and accountability, the Company remains committed to 
creating long-term value while contributing positively  
to the communities and regions in which it operates.

Annual Report 2025-26 6 7 Godawari Power & Ispat LimitedCORPORATE OVERVIEW
=== page 7 ===
An integrated, future-ready steel enterprise built on 
ore, energy and innovation
At Godawari Power and Ispat Limited, differentiation is 
not built on a single advantage — it is the outcome of 
a deliberately integrated model, decades of operational 
discipline and a bold expansion agenda that is already 
in execution. Here is what makes the Company 
structurally distinct.
Captive Iron Ore Security — From Mine to Mill
The Company operates two captive magnetite   
 iron ore mines with 165 Million tonnes of proven  
 reserves and 35+ years of mine life
Mining capacity more than doubled in 2025-26 to 
 6.70 MnT, following receipt of Consent to Operate 
 from the CECB for the Ari Dongri Mine 
 (2.35 MnT to 6 MnT)
Captive ore landed cost of approximately `2,900– 
 3,000 per tonne versus market price of `6,200 per  
 tonne — a structural cost advantage of over `3,000 
 per tonne that no merchant ore buyer can replicate
Full self-sufficiency in ore supply is targeted from 
 2027-28, once the 6 MnT beneficiation plant at 
 Ari Dongri is commissioned
India’s Only Gas-Based Pellet Plant — 
Premium Quality, Export-Ready
The Company operates a total pellet capacity of 
 4.70 MnT, including the newly commissioned 
 2 MnT plant — India’s first to use advanced natural 
 gas-based Grate-Kiln technology
High-grade ~65% Fe pellets command a realisation 
 premium of `1,000–1,500 per tonne over market  
 rates
Gas-based production reduces CO2 intensity by 
 approximately 200,000 tonnes per year versus 
 coal-based processes — making the Company’s 
 pellets compliant with the European Union’s Carbon 
 Border Adjustment Mechanism and positioning the 
 Company for export market access20 GWh BESS manufacturing facility  
 (commissioning March 2027): Tier-1 supply chain 
 fully secured; the Company is the only integrated 
 steel producer in India building a dedicated Battery 
 Energy Storage manufacturing plant
1 MnT Integrated Steel Plant (blast furnace route; 
 construction commencing October 2026): scales 
 long-product capacity 3x, targeting structural steel  
 and wire rods for India’s infrastructure sector
A Net-Cash Balance Sheet That Funds Growth 
Without Dilution
• The Company maintains a net cash position of 
 `837 Crores and generated operating cash flow of 
 `1,157 Crores in 2025-26 — the financial foundation 
 to execute a multi-thousand Crores capex cycle 
 without equity dilution
A conservative 1:1 debt-equity discipline is applied  
 to all major projects, including the Integrated Steel 
 Plant and BESS, preserving balance sheet resilience 
 through the expansion cycleEnd-to-End Integration Across the Steel Value Chain
The Company maintains seamless presence from 
 iron ore mining  beneficiation  pelletisation 
  sponge iron  steel billets  rolled products  
 galvanised fabricated structures and ferro alloys 
 with captive power generation.
Integration compresses per-unit production costs, 
 reduces supply-chain dependence and enables 
 margin stability even during soft realisation cycles 
 — demonstrated in 2025-26 with EBITDA and PAT 
 margins of 23% and 15%, respectively, despite 
 industry-wide pricing headwinds
The Company’s billets are approved by PGCIL for 
 power transmission projects, and the structural  
 rolled products division carries a six-month order  
 book, validating product quality at the premium 
 end of the market
Captive Renewable Power — Cost Leadership 
Through Clean Energy
Captive solar power capacity of 190 MW is 
 being expanded over 3x to 540 MW by 2027-28, 
 supplemented by 42 MW of waste heat recovery 
 power, 28.50 MW of biomass and 1.50 MW of wind
Every additional MW of captive solar directly 
 reduces the power cost for energy-intensive   
 operations, including the CRM Complex, pellet plant  
 and the upcoming Integrated Steel Plant
The adoption of an electric vehicle fleet — 
 comprising 10 EV dumpers, 24 EV loaders and 15 EV 
 excavators — has reduced mining transport 
 operating costs by approximately 75% and carbon 
 emissions by approximately 88% compared to 
 diesel vehicles
A `10,000+ Crores Growth Roadmap — 
All Projects in Execution
0.70 MnT CRM Complex (commissioning March 
 2027): enters premium coated flat steel — ZAM,  
 galvalume and colour-coat — registered under 
 PLI 1.1 and PLI 1.2 incentive schemesResponsible Growth — ESG as a Competitive Lever, 
Not a Compliance Exercise
The Company’s CO2 intensity stands at 
 ~3.39 tCO2 per tonne of steel (2025-26,  
 independently assured by SGS under  WSA and   
 CBAM standards) — competitive against   
 European Union basic oxygen steelmaking   
 benchmarks
A Carbon Capture and Utilisation pilot has been 
 commissioned with IIT Mumbai; biomass co-firing 
 trials are underway; ISO 26000 and ISO 27001:2022 
 certifications were achieved in 2025-26
The Company is committed to achieving Net Zero 
 Carbon Emissions by 2050 — a goal that is 
 structured, measurable and already in motion

KEY HIGHLIGHTS OF FINANCIAL YEAR 2025-26
THE YEAR THAT 
MOVED US FORWARD
Financial Performance at a Glance
FY 2025-26 has been a year of operational resilience 
for the Company. Despite softer realisations across the 
steel value chain, Godawari Power and Ispat Limited 
delivered a stable set of consolidated financials, with 
EBITDA and Profit After Tax margins holding firm at 
25% and 20% respectively. The strong performance 
in Q4 FY 2025-26 — driven by the pellet plant ramp-
up, higher sales volumes and improved realisations 
— demonstrated the underlying earnings power of 
the integrated business model. The Company enters 
FY 2026-27 with a healthy net cash position of `837 
Crores and operating cash flow of `1,157 Crores, 
providing full headroom to fund the ongoing capital 
expenditure programme from internal accruals.
Operational Performance
The Company delivered a strong operational 
performance across all key product segments in 
FY 2025-26. Sponge iron, structural rolled products 
and ferro alloys surpassed their annual production 
targets, while iron ore mining, pellets and steel billets 
achieved 92%, 95% and 96% of target respectively. 
Q4 FY 2025-26 marked the first full quarter of 
operations at the new 2 MnT pellet plant, with 
pellet sales volumes rising 157% quarter-on-quarter. 
Structural rolled products recorded a 319% increase 
year-on-year, reflecting the completion of rolling mill 
modifications at RR Ispat in Q3 FY 2025-26.Regulatory and Capacity Milestones
The year under review was a landmark year for 
regulatory progress, with GPIL securing all critical 
approvals required to unlock its next phase of growth. 
The receipt of Consent to Operate (CTO) for the Ari 
Dongri Mines at an enhanced capacity of 6 MnT 
was the single most important de-risking event of 
the year, converting iron ore from the Company’s 
biggest operational constraint into its most durable 
competitive advantage.
Consents to Operate (CTO) received during 
FY 2025-26
Ari Dongri Iron Ore Mines  —  Capacity enhanced 
from 2.35 MnT to 6 MnT (CECB approval received 
February 2026). Phased ramp-up under way; full-scale 
operations targeted from FY 2027-28.
 Sponge Iron (DRI) Division  —  Capacity enhanced 
 from 0.594 MnT to 0.65 MnT
 HB Wire Division  —  Capacity enhanced from 
 0.1 MnT to 0.115 MnT
 Waste Heat Recovery Power Plant  —  Additional 
 7 MW approved, taking total WHRP capacity to 
 49 MW
 Beneficiation Plant – Ari Dongri  —  Environment 
 Clearance and Consent to Set Up (CTS) received 
 on 31 January 2026 for expansion from 2.35 MnT 
 to 6 MnT. Commissioning targeted Q3 FY 2026-27Strategic Growth Initiatives
The Board of Directors approved four major strategic initiatives during FY 2025-26, each of which represents 
a distinct value-creation lever. Together, they form the Company’s transformational growth roadmap towards 
a revenue target of approximately `30,000 Crores by 2031.
New plant commissioned
 2 MnT Iron Ore Pellet Plant commissioned in   
 December 2025, taking total pellet capacity from 
 2.7 MnT to 4.7 MnT. The plant is India’s first to 
 use advanced natural gas-based Grate-Kiln 
 technology, replacing coal gas and achieving a 
 significant reduction in CO2 intensity

ESG and Decarbonisation
The Company made material progress on its ESG and 
decarbonisation agenda in FY 2025-26. Five energy-
efficiency projects were completed during the year, 
including the fuel switch from coal gas to natural gas 
in the new pellet plant. The Company maintained a CO₂ 
intensity of approximately 2.50 tCO₂ per tonne of steel 
on a CBAM basis — independently assured by SGS 
— and continued its progress towards the Net Zero 
Carbon Emissions goal by 2050.
Key ESG highlights
 CO2 intensity: 2.50 tCO2 per tonne of steel (CBAM 
 basis; FY 2025-26 average, assured by SGS). CO2  
 intensity on WSA basis: 2.35 tCO2 per tonne of 
 steel
 Natural gas fuel switch in the new 2 MnT pellet 
 plant — India’s first plant of this type — delivers 
 a CO2 reduction of approximately 2 Lakh tonnes 
 per annum versus coal-based processes
 Carbon Capture and Utilisation (CCU) pilot of 3 TPD 
 commissioned in collaboration with IIT Mumbai; 
 5 TPD replication planned at GPIL
 Biomass co-firing: successful trials with SALDOC 
 pellet at 15% feed mix in the biomass power plant

Overview
Godawari Power and Ispat Limited operates 
an end-to-end integrated steel and energy 
platform, with a comprehensive product 
portfolio spanning the entire value chain — 
from captive iron ore mining and high-grade 
pellet production through to finished steel 
products, ferro alloys and renewable power 
generation. Each product in the portfolio is 
produced with captive raw materials, captive 
power and a commitment to quality that 
has earned the Company recognition from 
India’s most demanding institutional buyers, 
including Power Grid Corporation of India 
Limited.
The product portfolio is structured to 
serve three broad end-use segments: 
the construction and infrastructure sector 
(which accounts for the majority of steel 
consumption in India), the power transmission 
and renewable energy sector (where the 
Company holds PGCIL certification), and 
the industrial and steelmaking sector (where 
captive and merchant supply of intermediates 
supports value chain integration).Iron Ore Pellets 4.70 MnT `9,724 per tonne Steelmaking, DRI, exports 
Sponge Iron (DRI) 0.65 MnT `26,232 per tonne Captive steelmaking, merchant SMS

=== page 13 ===
High-grade magnetite iron ore pellets with 
approximately 65% iron content, produced at the 
Company’s 4.70 MnT pellet facility at Siltara, Raipur. 
The newly commissioned 2 MnT plant — India’s first 
to utilise advanced natural gas-based Grate-Kiln 
technology — produces pellets that are CBAM-
compliant and export-ready. High-grade pellets 
command a realisation premium of ` 1,000 to  
`1,500 per tonne over market rates.Steel billets are semi-finished long steel products 
manufactured at GPIL Steel Melting Shop by melting 
sponge iron in induction furnaces and casting into 
square cross-section bars. The Company’s billets are 
approved by Power Grid Corporation of India Limited 
(PGCIL) for use in transmission projects, validating 
product quality at par with leading steel producers.
Direct Reduced Iron (DRI), commonly referred to as 
sponge iron, is produced by reducing high-grade iron 
ore pellets using coal as a reductant in rotary kilns. 
It is an intermediate product that serves as a clean, 
consistent metallic feed for the Company’s electric 
arc and induction furnaces.GPIL converts billets into three categories of finished 
long steel products at its rolling mills in Siltara 
and Urla Industrial Area. M.S. Rounds are used in 
construction and general engineering applications. 
H.B. (Hard Bright) Wires are precision-drawn wires 
used in pre-stressed concrete applications. Structural 
Rolled Products, produced at RR Ispat (the Company’s 
acquired structural rolling mill), include angles, 
channels, beams and joists used in industrial and 
infrastructure fabrication.Iron Ore PelletsSteel Billets

=== page 14 ===
Galvanised Fabricated Products represent the 
highest value-added segment in the Company’s 
steel portfolio. The Fabrication Shop at 
Urla Industrial Area converts rolled steel 
sections and plates into precision-engineered 
structures, which are then hot-dip galvanised 
to provide corrosion resistance. GPIL is one of 
a small number of Indian steel producers to 
hold PGCIL approval for supply of galvanised 
structures to power transmission projects.Through its captive renewable power portfolio 
GPIL provides a structural cost and sustainability 
advantage across all manufacturing operations. 
Power generated from solar, waste heat 
recovery (WHR), biomass and wind sources 
reduces dependence on grid electricity, 
lowers per-unit energy cost and progressively 
reduces the Company’s carbon intensity. The 
captive power infrastructure underpins GPIL 
commitment to achieving Net Zero Carbon 
Emissions by 2050.
GPIL manufactures silico-manganese ferro 
alloys through its group entities at Urla 
Industrial Area, Raipur. Ferro alloys are critical 
additives in the steelmaking process, used 
to achieve precise chemical compositions, 
improve strength, toughness and corrosion 
resistance. Silico-manganese is the primary 
deoxidiser and desulphuriser used in electric 
arc and induction furnace steelmaking.Galvanised

•  Power transmission towers: lattice structures for 
 66 kV to 765 kV high-voltage transmission lines 
 under PGCIL and state utility projects
•  Solar mounting structures: galvanised steel frames 
 for utility-scale ground-mounted solar power parks
•  Telecom towers and railway electrification masts
•  Industrial structures: crane girders, mezzanine floors 
 and heavy-duty structural frames
•  PGCIL approval provides access to specification- 
 driven, premium-priced demand with high entry 
 barriersApplications and end-use
•  Solar power (190 MW operational): generated from 
 parks at Rajnandgaon (70 MW), Maharumkala 
 (24 MW), Bemetara (52 MW HFAL), Tulsipur (18 MW) 
 and the newly commissioned 25 MW plant 
 (May 2026)
•  Waste Heat Recovery (WHR) power (49 MW): 
 generated from waste gases from pellet cooler and 
 ferro alloy operations, converting process waste 
 into electricity
•  Biomass power (28.5 MW): produced at Mahasamund 
 plant using agricultural residues; co-firing trials with 
 SALDOC pellet at 15% feed mix are under way
•  Wind power (1.50 MW): Karnataka location
•  Captive power reduces electricity cost, improves 
 CO2 intensity and supports CBAM-compliant pellet 
 production for export markets
Applications and end-use
•  Silico-manganese is used as a deoxidising,   
 desulphurising and alloying agent in all grades of 
 steel produced in electric arc and induction furnaces

 Steel Melting Shop ensures consistent steel quality 
 and supply security
•  Merchant sales to third-party steel producers in 
 Chhattisgarh and neighbouring states
•  Ferro alloy realisations have demonstrated resilience, 
 recording a 5% increase in FY 2025-26 against 
 a backdrop of softening steel prices

Dear Shareholders,
I am pleased to present 
the 27th Annual Report of 
Godawari Power and Ispat 
Limited for FY 2025-26. 
Guided by this year’s theme, 
“Charting the Next Horizon,” 
the Company achieved 
significant milestones 
during the year, including 
expansion of mining capacity, 
commissioning of new pellet 
capacity, advancement 
of renewable energy 
projects and progress on 
strategic growth initiatives, 
strengthening our position 
as an integrated and future-

B.L. Agrawal  
Chairman & Managing DirectorMACRO-ECONOMIC LANDSCAPE
The global economy remained resilient during FY 
2025-26 despite ongoing geopolitical tensions, 
evolving trade dynamics and commodity market 
volatility. Amid this backdrop, India continued to 
outperform major economies, driven by strong domestic 
consumption, sustained infrastructure investments and 
a growing manufacturing sector. With GDP growth of 
approximately 7.7% and inflation contained at around 
3.4%, the country reinforced its position as the world’s 
fastest-growing major economy.
Towards the end of the year, escalating geopolitical 
tensions and the Middle East crisis led to higher crude 
oil and energy prices, creating near-term inflationary 
concerns. Nevertheless, India’s long-term growth 
outlook remains robust, supported by strong economic 
fundamentals, favourable demographics and continued 
focus on infrastructure-led development.  
7% 
India remained the world’s fastest-growing major 
economy in FY 2025-26
3.4%  
Inflation remained largely under control despite global 
uncertainties
 
INDUSTRIAL OPPORTUNITY
India’s industrial growth story continues to create 
significant opportunities for the steel sector. Rising 
investments in infrastructure, transportation, renewable 
energy and manufacturing are expected to drive 
sustained demand for steel and value-added products 
in the years ahead. The Union Budget FY 2025-26 
allocated `11.21 lakh crore towards capital expenditure, 
while India’s crude steel production reached 168.4 MT, 
reinforcing the country’s position as one of the fastest-
growing steel markets globally. 
Simultaneously, India’s renewable energy transition is 
creating new opportunities across power transmission, 
energy storage and specialised steel applications. As 
the country expands its renewable energy capacity and 
strengthens grid infrastructure, demand for structural 
steel, galvanised products, pellets and energy storage 
solutions is expected to remain robust. Backed by 
captive iron ore resources, an integrated value chain 
and strategic investments in pelletisation, renewable 
energy, coated steel and battery energy storage 
systems, GPIL is well positioned to capitalise on these 
emerging opportunities and participate in India’s next 
phase of industrial growth.  `11+ Lakh Crore  
Investments Infrastructure by government
168.4 MT  
India’s crude steel production in FY26  
 
 
TRANSLATING VISION INTO ACTION
At GPIL, growth is not measured merely by capacity 
additions or production milestones, but by our ability to 
continuously strengthen the foundations of a resilient 
and future-ready enterprise. Guided by this philosophy, 
we continued to make meaningful progress across our 
strategic priorities, reinforcing our integrated business 
model and enhancing our ability to create long-term 
stakeholder value.
The year witnessed several important milestones that 
have expanded our growth runway and strengthened 
our competitive positioning. The receipt of Consent 
to Operate for the Ari Dongri iron ore mines at an 
enhanced capacity of 6 MnT significantly improved 
our raw material security, while the commencement of 
commercial operations at the new 2 MnT pellet plant 
deepened value addition across the steel value chain.
We also continued to advance projects that will 
define GPIL ’s next phase of growth. Progress on the 
0.7 MnT Cold Rolling Mill Complex and the 20 GWh 
Battery Energy Storage System project reflects our 
commitment to diversifying into higher-value and 
future-oriented segments. At the same time, our 
renewable energy expansion programme moved forward 
steadily, supporting our objective of achieving 540 MW 
of solar power capacity and strengthening long-term 
cost competitiveness.

  accountability EXPANSION: BUILDING CAPACITY FOR  
THE NEXT HORIZON
To capitalise on emerging opportunities and strengthen 
its competitive position, GPIL is undertaking a series 
of strategic expansion initiatives across mining, steel, 
renewable energy and new-age businesses. The 
Company has enhanced the capacity of the Ari Dongri 
iron ore mine from 2.35 MnT to 6 MnT, while pellet 
capacity has increased from 2.7 MnT to 4.7 MnT with 
the commissioning of the new 2 MnT pellet plant. 
Simultaneously, GPIL is progressing the development of 
a 0.7 MnT Cold Rolling Mill Complex, a 20 GWh Battery 
Energy Storage System (BESS) facility and expanding 
its renewable energy portfolio from 190 MW to 540 
MW. These investments are aimed at strengthening 
integration, increasing value addition, improving cost 
competitiveness and creating new growth avenues, 
supporting the Company’s aspiration of building a 
larger, more diversified and future-ready enterprise.
DELIVERING RESILIENT FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE
The strength of GPIL ’s integrated business model once 
again came to the fore during FY 2025-26. Despite 
softer realisations across key product categories and continued volatility in commodity markets, the 
Company delivered a resilient financial performance, 
supported by captive iron ore resources, operational 
efficiencies and prudent cost management.Revenue for the year remained stable at ` 5,381 Crores, 
while profitability continued to reflect the structural 
advantages of our business model. EBITDA stood at 
`1,253 Crores, translating into a healthy EBITDA margin 
of 23%, while Profit After Tax was ` 802 Crores with 
a PAT margin of 15%. The Company also maintained 
a strong net cash position of ` 837 Crores, providing 
the financial flexibility to pursue strategic growth 
opportunities while preserving balance sheet strength.
Our disciplined approach towards capital allocation, 
coupled with robust cash generation, continues to 
support ongoing expansion projects and reinforces 
our confidence in delivering sustainable value to 
stakeholders over the long term.

ADVANCING SUSTAINABLE AND  
RESPONSIBLE GROWTH
At GPIL, sustainability is not a parallel agenda—it is 
an integral part of our growth strategy. As we chart 
the next phase of expansion, we remain committed to 
balancing business performance with environmental 
stewardship, social responsibility and strong 
governance practices.
During the year, we continued to advance our 
decarbonisation journey through investments in 
renewable energy, energy efficiency and cleaner 
technologies. Our ongoing solar power expansion, 
development of battery energy storage capabilities and 
focus on resource optimisation reflect our commitment 
to building a future-ready and environmentally 
responsible enterprise. These initiatives support 
our long-term vision of achieving Net Zero Carbon 
Emissions by 2050 while enhancing operational 
resilience and competitiveness.
Beyond our operations, we remain deeply committed 
to creating a positive impact in the communities we 
serve. Through focused interventions in education, 
healthcare, skill development, rural infrastructure 
and community welfare, we continued to contribute 
towards inclusive and sustainable development. We 
also remain committed to fostering a safe, inclusive and performance-driven workplace while upholding 
the highest standards of governance, ethics and 
transparency.
WAY FORWARD
As we look ahead, we remain confident in the 
opportunities emerging from India’s infrastructure 
expansion, manufacturing growth and energy 
transition. Supported by a strong balance sheet, 
integrated operations and a clearly defined growth 
roadmap, GPIL is well positioned to participate in the 
country’s next phase of industrial development.
Our focus will remain on successfully commissioning 
and ramping up ongoing expansion projects, 
strengthening value-added product offerings, advancing 
renewable energy initiatives and building future growth 
platforms such as Battery Energy Storage Systems. At 
the same time, we will continue to drive operational 
excellence, enhance resource efficiency and maintain a 
disciplined approach to capital allocation.
ACKNOWLEDGEMENT
I would like to express my sincere gratitude to 
our employees, whose dedication, resilience and 
commitment continue to drive the Company’s success. I 
also extend my appreciation to our customers, suppliers, 
business partners, lenders and government authorities 
for their continued trust and support.
I am equally thankful to the communities around our 
operations, whose partnership remains integral to our 
journey of responsible and inclusive growth. Above 
all, I would like to thank our shareholders for their 
unwavering confidence in GPIL and their continued 
belief in our long-term vision.
As we continue Charting the Next Horizon, we remain 
committed to creating sustainable value, pursuing 
excellence and contributing meaningfully to India’s 
growth story.

BOARD OF DIRECTORS
THE STRENGTH 
BEHIND PROGRESS
Mr. B.L. Agrawal, aged 72, is a first-generation entrepreneur with 
nearly four decades of industry expertise. An Electronics Engineer by 
qualification, he founded Godawari Power & Ispat Limited (GPIL) and has 
been instrumental in shaping the Company’s strategic vision, operational 
scale, and integrated business framework.Mr. B. L. Agrawal 
Chairman & Managing Director

HOW THIS OPPORTUNITY SHAPES GPIL ’S STRATEGY:
India’s infrastructure cycle directly drives demand for GPIL ’s structural rolled products (PGCIL-approved),  
galvanised fabricated towers (approved for 66 kV to 765 kV transmission lines), H.B. Wires (pre-stressed concrete),  
M.S. Rounds (construction) and billets (re-rolling). The Company’s structural rolled products segment grew 781% 
year-on-year in FY 2025-26, demonstrating demand momentum that the 1 MnT Integrated Steel Plant is designed  
to capture at scale.
SUPPORTING EVIDENCE AND CONTEXT
 Railways: dedicated freight corridors, Vande Bharat expansion and station redevelopments create demand for  
 rails, structural steel and rolling stock; Budget FY 2025-26 allocated ` 2.52 Lakh Crore to railways
 Power transmission: India’s non-fossil fuel capacity reached 283 GW in FY 2025-26; continued grid expansion  
 generates demand for galvanised transmission towers, substations and structural steel
 Real estate and housing: PMAY-U 2.0 targets affordable housing; RBI House Price Index rose 4.2% in  
 Q4 FY 2025-26, supporting rebar and structural steel demand
 Telecom: 5G rollout across 99.9% of districts; network densification continues to require steel-intensive  
 tower infrastructure
 India regained net exporter status in steel for the first time since FY 2022-23, with exports rising 35.9% to  
 6.6 MT, reflecting domestic supply growth and quality improvementMARKET OPPORTUNITY
PATHWAYS TO 
THE NEXT HORIZON
Positioned at the convergence of multiple high-growth sectors, the business is well placed to benefit from India’s 
evolving industrial and economic landscape. The opportunities outlined below extend beyond cyclical market trends 
and are supported by structural drivers such as policy reforms, infrastructure investments, energy transition and 
shifting global supply chains. Collectively, they provide a strong platform for long-term value creation.
HOW THIS OPPORTUNITY SHAPES GPIL ’S STRATEGY:
GPIL is one of very few Indian steel companies with both captive iron ore (165 MnT reserves; 6.70 MnT capacity) and 
a large-scale pellet plant (4.70 MnT). The growing ore deficit structurally supports pellet demand and pricing. GPIL ’s 
expanded 4.70 MnT pellet capacity, with FY 2026-27 guidance of 4 MnT, positions the Company as a key merchant 
pellet supplier at a time of tightening domestic supply and rising imports.Why It Matters to GPIL

India’s Infrastructure-Led Steel 
Demand Supercycle 
The largest single growth engine for domestic 
steel consumption — government-led, 
multi-decade and acceleratingIron Ore and Pellet Supply Deficit 
— India’s Structural Raw Material 
Opportunity
India is the world’s second-largest crude steel producer and 
its fastest-growing major steel market. Crude steel production 
reached 168.4 MT in FY 2025-26, growing 10.7% year-on-
year, while finished steel consumption expanded to 164 MT. 
India is also the only major economy projected to accelerate 
steel demand growth — from +7.4% in 2026 to +9.2% in 2027 
(World Steel Association, April 2026) — at a time when China’s 
demand is expected to contract.
The Union Budget FY 2025-26 allocated `11.21 Lakh Crore in 
capital expenditure, directed at railways, highways, urban 
infrastructure, housing and logistics — the highest-steel-
consuming public investment categories. The PM Gati Shakti 
initiative integrates roads, rail, aviation and waterways into 
a unified infrastructure grid, creating sustained demand for 
structural steel, rebars, galvanised towers and rails across 
multiple years.India’s steel capacity is expanding rapidly towards 300 MnT by 
2031, but domestic iron ore production is not keeping pace. The 
current near-balanced supply position is expected to widen into 
an estimated 150 to 190 MnT iron ore deficit by 2031. India has 
already emerged as a net importer of iron ore, with imports rising 
from 0.05 MnT in 2023 to 10.50 MnT in 2026, driven by supply 
gaps in high-grade ore.
Iron ore pellet prices traded in a range of `8,500 to `10,000 per 
tonne through FY 2025-26, with current prices around ` 10,150 
per tonne. The pellet market continues to command a premium 
over iron ore fines, with high-grade 65% Fe pellets earning an 
additional `1,000 to ` 1,500 per tonne above market rates.India crude steel production FY26

SUPPORTING EVIDENCE AND CONTEXT
 India’s steel capacity is targeted to reach 300 MnT by 2031, requiring 450 to 500 MT of iron ore annually against  
 projected domestic production of only 290 to 310 MT — a structural supply gap of 150 to 190 MT
 Iron ore imports surged from 0.05 MnT in 2023 to 10.50 MnT in 2026, confirming that domestic supply is already  
 insufficient for existing capacity
 Captive iron ore at ` 2,900 per tonne versus merchant market price of ` 6,200 per tonne gives GPIL a cost   
 advantage of over ` 3,300 per tonne that compounds as demand and prices rise
 Iron ore accounted for 69.64% of India’s total metallic minerals value (` 1,09,623 Crores of ` 1,57,415 Crores   
 total) in FY 2025-26 — the dominant raw material in Indian mining by value
 The global EAF steelmaking market, growing at approximately 12.6% CAGR over 2026–2034 (Fortune Business  
 Insights), structurally underpins demand for DRI-grade pellets as a substitute for scrapSUPPORTING EVIDENCE AND CONTEXT
 CBAM full transition phase from January 2026 creates a carbon-cost differential on European steel imports; low- 
 carbon Indian pellets become competitively advantaged relative to high-carbon alternatives
 US 50% Section 232 tariffs divert Chinese and other steel exports away from the US market and towards Asia  
 and Europe, intensifying price competition in some segments while creating arbitrage opportunities in others
 DR-grade pellet export premium of approximately US$ 28 per tonne versus US$ 13 per tonne for BF-grade pellets  
 provides a significant incentive to upgrade pellet specifications for export (Q3 FY 2025-26 earnings call data)
 Export pellet price ex-plant is now close to domestic price, making the option commercially viable without yield  
 sacrifice — a direct consequence of the Company’s investment in gas-based technology
 India’s pellet export infrastructure is maturing; the Company’s Chhattisgarh location provides access to eastern  
 port connectivity for Middle East and European exports
HOW THIS OPPORTUNITY SHAPES GPIL ’S STRATEGY:
GPIL ’s new 2 MnT gas-based pellet plant is India’s only plant of its kind and is inherently CBAM-compliant. Its natural 
gas fuel reduces CO2 intensity by approximately 2 Lakh tonnes per annum versus coal-based routes. The Company 
holds a CO2 intensity of 2.50 tCO2 per tonne of steel (CBAM basis, FY 2025-26, assured by SGS) and management 
has confirmed export readiness. The Company has the flexibility to switch between domestic and export markets as 
pricing differentials evolve.HOW THIS OPPORTUNITY SHAPES GPIL ’S STRATEGY:
GPIL is entering this market as one of India’s earliest large-scale domestic BESS manufacturers, through Godawari 
New Energy Private Limited (100% subsidiary). The Company has already secured a complete Tier-1 supply chain 
— EVE Power (LFP cells), ROCHE Energy (BOS), FIMER India and Hopewind (PCS) — and received comprehensive 
Maharashtra government incentives. The Company’s CRM Complex (commissioning March 2027) will supply steel 
containers for BESS manufacture, creating an internal synergy that reduces BOS costs. The Vision 2031 revenue 
target from BESS is approximately ` 15,000 Crores at 16 GWh annual output.Opportunity OpportunityCBAM and Global Trade Realignment 
— Export Opportunity for Low-Carbon 
PelletsIndia’s Grid-Scale Battery Energy Storage 
Market — First-Mover Manufacturing 
Opportunity
The EU’s Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism and US Section 232 
tariffs are reshaping global steel trade flows, creating a premium 
export window for carbon-efficient Indian pellet producers 
The European Union’s Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism 
(CBAM) entered full operational phase from January 2026, 
imposing a carbon price on steel, iron ore, fertilisers, cement 
and other imports based on embedded CO2 intensity. 
Simultaneously, the United States reimposed steel tariffs to 
50% under Section 232 from April 2026, redirecting global 
steel trade flows and creating both threats and opportunities 
for Indian producers.
For low-carbon pellet producers, CBAM is a significant tailwind. 
High-grade, gas-produced pellets with lower embedded CO2 
qualify for lower CBAM charges than coal-based alternatives, 
and command a market premium. The Company’s management 
has confirmed that the export price differential with domestic 
prices has narrowed sufficiently to make export viable — 
“whenever domestic demand softens, pellet exports from 
the next quarter” are planned (Q4 FY 2025-26 Earnings Call,          
20 May 2026).India’s renewable energy ambition requires large-scale grid storage; 
domestic BESS manufacturing is at an inflection point with demand 
significantly outpacing supply
India’s installed renewable energy capacity reached 283.46 
GW in FY 2025-26, including 150.26 GW of solar. As 
intermittent renewable penetration rises, grid-scale Battery 
Energy Storage Systems (BESS) are becoming a critical 
infrastructure requirement for grid balancing and stability. 
The Government of India has launched dedicated BESS 
procurement auctions and the National Electricity Plan (NEP) 
envisages 236 GWh of grid storage by 2032 — representing 
a domestic BESS market of over ` 19 Lakh Crores at current 
pricing.
As of FY 2025-26, domestic BESS manufacturing capacity 
in India remains minimal. Most deployments depend on 
imported systems. BESS container prices in the Indian market 
have risen from approximately ` 70 Lakh per MWh to ` 80–85 
Lakh per MWh, with margins improving from the originally 
conceived 7 to 8% to approximately 12 to 13%.CBAM operational since

 government-funded projects, prioritising domestic coated steel producers over importers
HOW THIS OPPORTUNITY SHAPES GPIL ’S STRATEGY:
GPIL ’s 0.70 MnT CRM Complex — registered under both PLI 1.1 and PLI 1.2 — is designed specifically to serve 
premium coated steel demand with ZAM, galvalume, colour-coat and cold rolled coils. The plant is designed with an 
internal synergy: CRM output will supply steel containers for the Company’s BESS manufacturing facility, creating 
a guaranteed captive demand channel. Commissioning in March 2027, with 50% utilisation (3 to 3.5 Lakh tonnes) 
targeted in FY 2027-28 and 90%+ from FY 2028-29, positions GPIL ahead of most competitors in this segment.OpportunityPremium Coated Steel — Growing 
Demand for Value-Added Flat Products
India’s renewable energy, appliance, automotive and 
construction sectors are driving structural demand for coated 
flat steel, which currently depends heavily on imports
India’s coated flat steel market — comprising galvanised, 
ZAM (zinc-aluminium-magnesium), galvalume and colour-
coated products — is one of the fastest-growing segments of 
the domestic steel industry. Demand is driven by solar panel 
mounting structures, EV body panels, white goods and roofing 
applications. Domestic coated steel production has lagged 
capacity additions in end-use sectors, resulting in sustained 
import dependence.
The Production-Linked Incentive (PLI) Scheme 1.1 (` 6,322 
Crores outlay, FY 2025-26 to FY 2029-30) specifically targets 
coated steel, high-strength steel, specialty rails, alloy steel 
and electrical steel, providing production-linked incentives 
that improve unit economics for domestic manufacturers. The 
Ministry of Steel signed MoUs for 85 projects under the PLI 
scheme, with committed investments of `11,887 Crores and 8.7 
MnT of planned capacity.PLI scheme outlay (Specialty Steel)

STRATEGIC REVIEW
POWERING THE NEXT 
GROWTH CHAPTER
Godawari Power and Ispat Limited pursues a clear, integrated and sequenced growth strategy, structured around 
five interlocking pillars. Together, these pillars are designed to grow the Company from a revenue of `5,381 Crores 
in FY 2025-26 to approximately `30,000 Crores by 2031 — a 5x expansion anchored in projects that are already 
approved, land-acquired, equipment-ordered and financing-secured. The strategy is guided by the founding belief 
of Serving Society through Industry, with responsible growth driven by operational excellence, innovation and 
sustainability at its core.

Green Diversification 
Entering Clean Energy Manufacturing and Non-Ferrous RecyclingKEY INITIATIVES AND ACTIONS
KEY INITIATIVES AND ACTIONSThe Company’s second strategic pillar is to migrate from commodity intermediates towards premium, specification-
driven steel products with structurally higher margins and greater resilience to commodity price cycles. This is being 
executed through two transformational projects: the 0.70 MnT Cold Rolled and Coated Steel Complex (CRM Complex) 
and the 1 MnT Integrated Steel Plant (ISP), both of which leverage captive raw material supply and infrastructure 
already in place.
The Company is pursuing a disciplined green diversification strategy that leverages its existing engineering, 
manufacturing and supply-chain capabilities to enter two high-growth, low-carbon adjacencies: Battery Energy 
Storage System (BESS) manufacturing and non-ferrous metal recycling. Both initiatives position the Company at the 
intersection of India’s energy transition and circular economy trends, offering revenue diversification beyond the steel 
cycle.Strategy

Vision 2031 revenue target of approximately 
`30,000 Crores (5x from FY 2025-26) is 
anchored entirely in projects that are approved, 
land-acquired, equipment-ordered and 
financing-arranged — representing a credible, 
execution-ready roadmap rather than an 
aspirational projectionDecarbonisation Drive 
Achieving Net Zero by 2050 Through Solar Scale-Up, EV Transition and Carbon Technology
Financial Discipline and Governance Excellence 
Funding Transformational Growth from Internal Accruals Without Compromising Balance Sheet StrengthKEY INITIATIVES AND ACTIONS
KEY INITIATIVES AND ACTIONSDecarbonisation is both an ethical commitment and a strategic imperative for the Company. The shift to cleaner energy 
reduces operating costs, improves carbon intensity, unlocks access to CBAM-compliant export markets and strengthens 
the Company’s ESG positioning with institutional investors and customers alike. The strategy rests on four concurrent 
pillars: captive solar expansion, EV fleet transition in mining, carbon capture and utilisation (CCU) technology, and 
biomass co-firing.
The fifth strategic pillar is the financial and governance architecture that makes all the others possible. The Company’s 
approach to capital allocation is deliberately conservative: every major project is approved within a 1:1 debt-equity 
guardrail, with equity funded from operating cash flows rather than equity dilution. This discipline has enabled the 
Company to embark on a ` 10,000+ Crore multi-year capex programme while maintaining a net cash balance sheet and 
continuing to pay dividends.Strategy
Strategy

GPIL EXPANSION PLAN
STRENGTHENING THE 
FOUNDATIONS OF FUTURE
GPIL is executing the most significant capital expansion in its 25-year history. Six 
concurrent projects — spanning iron ore mining, pellet production, cold rolled steel,  
a 1 MnT integrated steel plant, a 20 GWh battery energy storage system and captive 
solar power — are at various stages of construction and commissioning across  
FY26–FY30. Each project carries a dedicated capex outlay and a target commissioning 
date. Together, they move GPIL from a mining-led pellet producer to a fully integrated, 
diversified steel and clean energy company.

=== page 27 ===
FY26 EXECUTION
Across six product lines, GPIL delivered at or above FY26 guidance in four — sponge iron (109%), ferro alloys (103%), 
rolled products (112%) and steel billets (96%). Iron ore mining reached 92% of guidance, with the capacity ramp 
constrained by CTO timing at Ari Dongri (received February 2026). Pellet production reached 95% of guidance in  
a year that included commissioning of a 2 MnT new plant in December 2025.
FY27 guidance reflects the first full year of the 2 MnT pellet plant (targeting 85% utilisation vs. 78% in Q4FY26) and 
the commencement of Ari Dongri ramp-up from 2.35 MnT to 6 MnT. Iron ore mining guidance jumps 24% YoY to 3.4 
MnT — the first tranche of the phased expansion. Sponge iron guidance holds flat at 0.65 MnT, consistent with the 
CTO received for the division (0.594   0.65 MnT). Rolled products guidance of 0.44 MnT reflects continued ramp-up 
of a division that ran at just 21,499 MT in FY25.
GPIL received Consent to Operate from the CECB 
for Ari Dongri mines at enhanced capacity of 6 MnT 
in February 2026 — more than doubling the mine’s 
permitted output. The expansion adds 3.65 MnT of 
incremental mining capacity, moving total captive ore 
security to 6.7 MnT (Ari Dongri 6 MnT + Boria Tibu 
0.7 MnT). Phased ramp-up begins in FY27; full-scale 
operations are targeted from FY28.
Alongside mine expansion, an ore quality upgrade 
is underway: a beneficiation plant at the mine site 
(0.6 MnT in FY26, scaling to 6 MnT) is targeted for 
commissioning by Q3 FY27. This shifts beneficiation 
closer to the ore source, reduces transport of gangue 
material, and improves ore grade entering the pellet 
plant — consolidating the quality advantage that 
underpins the ` 1,000–1,500/ton pellet premium.The 2 MnT pellet plant was commissioned in December 
2025, expanding total pellet capacity by 74% from  
2.7 MnT to 4.7 MnT. Q4FY26 was the first full quarter 
post-commissioning; capacity utilisation reached 
78% and is guided to 85% in FY27. Total FY26 pellet 
production was 2.86 MnT (95% of guidance); pellet 
sales volumes grew 15% YoY to 18,80,815 MT.
This facility is the first in India to deploy natural gas-
based Grate-Kiln pelletisation technology — replacing 
the conventional coal gas process used in GPIL ’s existing 
2.7 MnT plant. The fuel switch enables approximately 2 
lakh tonnes per year of CO2 reduction. The technology, 
combined with GPIL ’s ~65% Fe magnetite ore, produces 
higher-grade pellets that command a premium of 
`1,000–1,500/ton over market-priced pellets.
The CRM Complex is GPIL ’s first move into value-added 
cold rolled, galvanized, and coated steel — products 
that command significantly higher realisations than 
hot-rolled or semi-finished outputs. The 0.7 MnT 
complex will manufacture Cold Rolled Steel products 
across multiple coating lines.
Planned capex is ` 900 crore, funded through ` 550 
crore debt (bank sanction received) and the balance 
from internal accruals. Land acquisition of 452 acres 
(shared with ISP) is complete. On-site construction is 
expected to commence July 2026, with commissioning 
scheduled for March 2027. The project is registered 
under PLI 1.1 and PLI 1.2 incentive schemes.
Equipment orders have been placed for all key 
process lines: Pickling Line, CGL Line (GL/ZAM), CGL 
Line (ZAM/Alu Silicon/GI), Colour Coating Line and 
Acid Regeneration Plant. Advance payments have 
been released and detailed engineering/design was 
completed progressively through April 2026. Equipment 
drawing approvals were expected by June 2026.Project Deep-DivesIron Ore Pellet Plant Expansion — 2 MnT

=== page 28 ===
GPIL has approved the establishment of a 1 MnT 
Integrated Steel Plant for structural steel and wire rods 
— tripling the company’s structural rolled steel capacity 
from the existing base of approximately 0.5 MnT. The 
ISP will be a fully integrated facility, drawing on captive 
iron ore from the expanded Ari Dongri mines and 
captive power from the 250 MW solar project approved 
for co-location.
Total capex is planned at ` 7,000 crore, funded through 
a 1:1 debt-to-equity mix using internal accruals. The 
452-acre land parcel (shared with the CRM Complex) 
has been acquired. Environment approval has been 
granted; consent to set up is awaited. Equipment 
supplier negotiations are in progress. Construction is 
scheduled to commence October 2026.GPIL is expanding captive solar power capacity from 165 MW to 540 MW across three project tranches. The 25 MW 
tranche has been commissioned and synchronised with the grid. The 100 MW tranche is in module installation, with 
completion targeted for Q2 FY27. The 250 MW tranche — approved specifically to power the ISP and CRM complex — 
is in early construction with partial land in possession; government land allotment of the balance expected by  
June 2026.
The 540 MW target covers power requirements for the iron ore mines, the 2 MnT additional pellet plant, the CRM 
Complex, and the 1 MnT Integrated Steel Plant. A 45 MWh battery storage system (separate from the commercial 
BESS project) is also under implementation for the captive solar plants, with commissioning targeted by August 2026. 
Total capex incurred across all solar tranches as at 31 March 2026: ` 268 crore (` 72 Cr + ` 107 Cr + ` 89 Cr).
Why solar expansion is tied to steel expansion
The 250 MW solar project is specifically approved to meet power requirements for the CRM Complex and 1 MnT 
ISP — the same facilities being built on the 452-acre land parcel. Solar power reduces long-run energy cost for 
both projects and supports GPIL ’s Net Zero 2050 target.The 20 GWh BESS project marks GPIL ’s first 
diversification beyond steel and power into clean 
energy infrastructure manufacturing. The project is 
being executed through Godawari New Energy Private 
Limited, a wholly owned subsidiary, at AURIC Industrial 
Area, Sambhajee Nagar (Aurangabad), Maharashtra.
All major supply chain agreements have been executed: 
EVE Power Co. Ltd. for Grade-1 628 Ah LFP cells (long-
term), Shanghai Shenyi Roche Energy Technology 
Ltd. for Balance of System supply, FIMER India and 
Hopewind for Power Conversion System supply and 
Lead Intelligent Wuxi for BESS assembly equipment. 
Factory Acceptance Testing is expected by August 
2026. The Maharashtra government has approved 
multiple project incentives including electricity duty 
and stamp duty exemptions, power tariff subsidy, 
interest and capital subsidies, SGST-linked incentives 
and ESI/EPF reimbursements.
Land of 112 acres has been acquired; soil testing is 
complete; compound wall construction is underway. 
EMS vendor has been finalised from India; testing 
equipment finalised from REPPOWER and NEWARE, 
China. Planned capex: ` 1,400 crore (1:1 debt-equity). 
Commissioning is targeted for March 2027.Integrated Steel Plant — 1 MnT

=== page 30 ===
REVENUE STABILITY IN A SOFT-PRICING ENVIRONMENT
Revenue stood at `5,381 Crores in FY 2025-26, remaining virtually unchanged year-on-year (+0.1%), despite a 3 
to 10% decline in realisations across most product categories. Volume growth — particularly in iron ore pellets 
(+15% YoY) and rolled structural products (+781% YoY) — offset the pricing headwind, validating the Company’s 
integrated and volume-led growth model.
SUSTAINED EBITDA PROFITABILITY WITH MARGIN EXPANSION
EBITDA grew 5% year-on-year to `1,253 Crores in FY 2025-26, with the margin improving to 25% from 22% in 
FY 2024-25. Q4 FY 2025-26 was particularly strong, with EBITDA reaching `439 Crores (+91% quarter-on-quarter, 
+38% year-on-year) as the new 2 MnT pellet plant ramped up and iron ore mining volumes surged following receipt 
of the Consent to Operate for the Ari Dongri Mine.
NET CASH BALANCE SHEET — A STRUCTURAL COMPETITIVE ADVANTAGE
The Company closed FY 2025-26 with a net cash position of `837 Crores (gross cash and bank balances of ` 1,145 
Crores against total debt of `427 Crores). Net worth expanded to `5,849 Crores — a 3.9x increase over FY 2019-
20 — reflecting consistent profit reinvestment and balance sheet strengthening. The debt-to-equity ratio stands at 
0.07x, one of the lowest in the mid-cap steel sector.
STRONG AND GROWING OPERATING CASH FLOW
Cash flow from operating activities (CFO) grew 29% year-on-year to `1,157 Crores in FY 2025-26, demonstrating the 
quality of earnings and efficient working capital management. This robust CFO is sufficient to fund the Company’s 
planned FY 2026-27 capital expenditure of `1,500 to `2,000 Crores entirely from internal accruals, without 
recourse to additional equity.
CONSISTENT SHAREHOLDER VALUE CREATION ACROSS THE CYCLE
The Company has grown net worth from `1,503 Crores in FY 2019-20 to `5,849 Crores in FY 2025-26, 
compounding at approximately 25% per annum over six years. Profit After Tax (PAT) CAGR over FY 2019-20 to 
FY 2025-26 stands at 29%. The Board recommended a dividend of Re. 1 per share (i.e. 100% of Face Value of 
Re.1/=) for FY 2025-26, reinforcing the commitment to shareholder returns even during an intensive capital 
expenditure phase.FINANCIAL CAPITAL
THE FINANCIAL FOUNDATION 
OF GROWTH
Over the past decade, a combination of captive raw material security, integrated operations and prudent financial 
management has enabled the creation of a strong and resilient financial foundation. Despite softer realisations 
across the steel value chain, GPIL delivered stable earnings, operating cash flow of `1,157 Crores, a net cash 
position of `837 Crores and an EBITDA margin of 25%, providing the financial strength to support the Company’s 
`10,000+ Crores multi-year expansion programme.Net Sales (`Crores)

 Solar power: 190 MW installed as at May 2026 (165 MW existing across Rajnandgaon 70 MW,

 Wind power: 1.50 MW at Karnataka location
 Captive power reduces electricity cost by approximately `3 to `5 per unit compared to grid tariff, 
 delivering annualised savings of approximately `200 to `300 Crores at current capacity; expanding 
 to 540 MW will amplify savings further Coal-based DRI rotary kiln process; uses captive high-grade pellets as iron ore feed, ensuring input quality 
 and supply security
 DRI is the primary metallic charge for the Company’s Steel Melting Shop, eliminating dependence on 
 scrap and ensuring consistent steel quality
 CTO received from CECB for capacity enhancement from 0.594 MnT to 0.65 MnT during FY 2025-26;

At Godawari Power and Ispat Limited, people are the cornerstone of operational

LEARNING AND DEVELOPMENT
Capability building is a continuous priority. During FY 2025-26, the Company conducted an extensive training and 
awareness programme spanning all units — the Siltara integrated plant and R.R. Ispat — covering over 10,962 
participations across management employees, permanent workers, and contractual workers. All 1,659 permanent 
employees and all 2,070 permanent workers received 100% coverage on both health and safety and skill-
upgradation training, consistent with GPIL ’s BRSR commitments.
Programmes spanned workplace safety, fire prevention, occupational health and hygiene, behaviour-based 
safety, environmental awareness, information security management (ISO 27001:2022), energy management (ISO 
50001:2018), HR systems (Spine-HR), and ethics and governance (anti-bribery, human rights, POSH). Safety 
induction training covered 7,295 participants (company and contractor combined) during the year, while a Job 
Safety Training classroom programme reached 1,193 participants across all plant divisions. A notable highlight was 
the ISO 31000 and COSO ERM risk management masterclass delivered by SGS Global Academy (17–18 April 2026), 
training 65+ employees and marking a strategic shift towards a proactive, risk-aware decision-making culture.RETIREMENT BENEFITS — FY 2025-26

OCCUPATIONAL HEAL TH AND SAFETY
Safety is a non-negotiable tenet of GPIL ’s operational philosophy. The Company has implemented a robust 
Occupational Health and Safety Management System (OHSMS) certified to ISO 45001:2018, encompassing 
hazard identification and risk assessment, legal compliance monitoring, safety training, incident reporting and 
investigation, emergency preparedness, and continuous performance measurement across all facilities.KEY SAFETY INITIATIVES IN FY 2025-26
 Apex Safety Committee reconstituted under Section 41(G) of the Factories Act, 1948 and Rule 73(i) of the 
 MP/CG Rules, 1962, with equal representation of management and non-management members
 Six Safety Line Walk Teams (SLWTs) formed, each led by senior cross-divisional members, conducting daily 
 walks from 3:00 PM to 4:30 PM — 90 line walks held with 3,474 observations recorded (27 January - 
 10 May 2026); 1,758 observations closed
 Centralised Integrated Control Room (ICR) established for 24×7 monitoring of plant operations, 
 PPE compliance, geo-fencing, and unauthorised access, powered by AI-enabled surveillance cameras
 GPIL Safety Captains designated to drive safety culture ownership at the plant level
 Competent Person engaged for scaffolding inspection, Green Tag certification, and specialised safety training
 Work-at-Height training and rescue demonstrations conducted in collaboration with M/s Karam
 55th National Safety Week celebrated with plant-wide awareness events and demonstrations
 Mock Drill conducted at the 1.8 MTPA Pellet Plant in the presence of the Assistant Director, Industrial Health 
 and Safety, DDIHS office (1 May 2026) — no major observations recorded
 Regular safety patrolling conducted to identify and mitigate human errors and behavioural deviations
EMPLOYEE RELATIONS AND GRIEVANCE REDRESSAL
The Company maintains a structured grievance redressal framework accessible to all categories of employees and 
workers — permanent and contractual alike — through the Grievance Redressal Mechanism, the POSH Committee, 
the Safety and Works Committee, and the Whistle-Blower Mechanism. All employees and workers have access to 
non-occupational medical and healthcare services. The Company’s premises comply with the Rights of Persons 
with Disabilities Act, 2016, and an equal opportunity policy embedded within the Human Rights Policy reinforces 
GPIL ’s commitment to inclusive workplaces.
ETHICS, HUMAN RIGHTS AND COMPLIANCE
GPIL ’s human capital governance is underpinned by an interlocking set of policies promoting ethical conduct and 
human rights. The Anti-Corruption and Anti-Bribery Policy prohibits any form of bribery, corruption, facilitation 
payments, or unethical practices in dealings with all stakeholders. The Human Rights Policy extends equal 
opportunity to differently abled persons in compliance with the Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016. 
The POSH framework is operational across all locations, with awareness sessions held during the year covering 
34 participants (23 male, 11 female). No POSH complaints were pending at year end. Training on anti-bribery, 
human rights, and affirmative action was conducted for management employees and workers across all units 
during FY 2025-26.

INTELLECTUAL CAPITAL
BUILDING THE FOUNDATION 
FOR THE NEXT HORIZON
Technology and innovation continue to strengthen operational excellence and long-
term competitiveness. During FY 2025-26, the Company advanced its digital and 
process capabilities through the commissioning of India’s first gas-based Grate-Kiln 
pellet plant, deployment of AI-enabled surveillance systems, attainment of new ISO 
certifications and implementation of an enterprise-wide risk management framework.The most significant intellectual capital event of FY 2025-26 was the commissioning of India’s first and only 
natural gas-based Grate-Kiln pellet plant at Siltara in December 2025. This technology decision — choosing natural 
gas over coal gasification — represented a deliberate intellectual bet on decarbonisation, CBAM compliance and 
export optionality. By adopting a technology route that no other Indian pellet producer operates at scale, the 
Company secured a competitive differentiation that cannot be replicated quickly.
GAS-BASED GRATE-KILN PELLET PLANT — TECHNOLOGY HIGHLIGHTS
 India’s first and only natural gas-based Grate-Kiln iron ore pellet plant: 2 MnT capacity, taking total installed 
 capacity to 4.70 MnT
 Natural gas replaces coal gas as the process fuel: 64% reduction in CO₂ emissions per unit of pellet output; 
 total CO2 saving approximately 2 Lakh tonnes per annum
 7-year natural gas supply MoU with GAIL India Limited; first long-term gas linkage for an Indian pellet producer 
 for this specific application
 Capital saving of approximately `100 Crores versus the coal gasification route; the intellectual choice of 
 technology delivered both an environmental and a financial outcome
 CBAM-compliant production: lower CO2 embedded in pellets qualifies for lower carbon border charges under 
 EU’s Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (fully operational since January 2026)
 Export-ready architecture: management has confirmed that pellet exports to Middle East and European 
 markets will commence when domestic price differentials make export commercially optimal
 DR-pellet upgrade optionality: the gas-based plant provides the platform to upgrade to DR-grade pellets (CCS 
 270+) which command an export premium of approximately US$ 28 per tonne vs US$ 13 per tonne for BF-gradeISO / quality

    costInitiative Partner / Forum Status (FY 2025-26) Strategic Significance   GPIL actively pursues R&D through structured academic partnerships and industry consortia, embedding external 
knowledge into its operational and environmental strategy. FY 2025-26 saw the commissioning of a Carbon 
Capture and Utilisation (CCU) pilot unit and the deepening of hydrogen steelmaking research, positioning the 
Company at the frontier of low-carbon steel technology in India.The Company operationalised a centralised Integrated Control Room (ICR) during FY 2025-26, consolidating 
plant safety, security, vehicular management and operational monitoring into a single real-time digital command 
centre. This initiative reflects the Company’s belief that digital intelligence embedded in manufacturing operations 
reduces risk, lowers the cost of safety management and creates an auditable, data-driven governance environment.
INTEGRATED CONTROL ROOM (ICR) — CAPABILITIES
 24×7 monitoring of plant operations, safety compliance, security surveillance and vehicular movement across  
 all operational areas of the manufacturing complex
 AI-based smart surveillance cameras deployed at critical locations for real-time detection of unsafe acts, 
 PPE non-compliance, unauthorised access, abnormal activities and unauthorised vehicle movement
 Geo-fencing systems integrated with automated notification and alert mechanisms; the system enables 
 immediate action against safety violations, geo-fence breaches and security-related incidents without 
 manual monitoring
 The ICR platform processes and logs safety violations centrally, enabling trend analysis, pattern detection 
 and proactive intervention — transitioning safety management from reactive to predictive
 The digital surveillance infrastructure supports the reconstituted Apex Safety Committee’s mandate 
 under Section 41(G) of the Factories Act, 1948 for real-time safety compliance tracking
EV FLEET TECHNOLOGY — MINING OPERATIONS
 The Company’s deployment of electric vehicles across its mining operations represents a technology-led 
 transformation of a traditionally high-cost, high-carbon activity:
 Fleet deployed: 10 EV dumpers, 24 EV loaders and 15 EV excavators operational at Ari Dongri and 
 Boria Tibu mines in FY 2025-26
 Operating cost reduction: approximately 75% versus diesel-powered equivalents; CO2 emission reduction: 
 approximately 88%
 Long-term plan: full transition of approximately 350 mining trucks to an EV fleet, requiring capex of 
 approximately `350 Crores; expected to eliminate `500 to `600 Crores of diesel cost per annum 
 at full scale
 EV fleet data is being used to develop fleet utilisation models that will inform the sizing and sequencing 
 of the full electrification programme
CBAM EMISSIONS MONITORING DASHBOARD
 A dedicated GHG and CBAM emissions monitoring dashboard has been implemented to track CO₂ intensity 
 metrics in real-time against CBAM thresholds
 CO2 intensity reporting is independently assured by SGS under both WSA (World Steel Association) and 
 CBAM methodologies — the dual assurance is required for both statutory ESG reporting and export eligibility
 The dashboard supports management decision-making on product mix, fuel choices and export timing relative 
 to carbon border costsResearch & Development and Academic Collaborations

SOCIAL AND RELATIONSHIP CAPITAL
PARTNERSHIPS THAT 
POWER PROGRESS
Community Development
GPIL ’s operations span three Chhattisgarh districts — Raipur (Siltara integrated plant 
and city offices), Kanker (Kachhe Aridongri iron ore mines), and the Boria Tibu mine 
area. In FY26, GPIL directed ` 26.38 crore across 58+ CSR activities, reaching 4,56,131 
beneficiaries. Education was the largest thematic allocation at ` 7.47 crore (28%), 
followed by community welfare at ` 5.78 crore (22%) and health care at ` 5.68 crore (22%).
CSR expenditure is tracked unit-wise, with each facility managing investment relative to its local operational footprint. 
The Siltara plant accounts for 63.9% of total CSR spend, reflecting its larger scale and longer tenure. The Kachhe 
Aridongri mines contribute 28.4%, weighted heavily towards rural road infrastructure — the primary community  
need in that mining geography.

SUPPLIER ECOSYSTEM
Education and Skill Development  |  `746.70 Lakh
GPIL ’s supply chain has two structurally distinct segments: captive inputs — iron ore and a large share of power 
— fully secured within the company’s own asset base, and externally procured inputs — coal, silico manganese, 
capital equipment, and services — where supplier relationships govern cost and project delivery. In FY26, iron ore 
was sourced 100% from GPIL ’s own mines at a landed cost of approximately `2,900/ton against a market price of 
approximately `6,200/ton.

Annual Report 2025-26 72 73 Godawari Power & Ispat LimitedCORPORATE OVERVIEW
=== page 40 ===
CAPITAL EQUIPMENT SUPPLIERS — ACTIVE CAPEX CYCLE
FY26 placed the highest capital equipment procurement demand in GPIL ’s recent history, with three major projects 
— the 0.7 MnT CRM Complex (`900 crore), the 20 GWh BESS project (`1,400 crore) and the 250 MW captive solar 
expansion — simultaneously in procurement and construction phases. Multi-year supply agreements now govern a 
significant share of future capital inputs.CUSTOMER RELATIONSHIPS 
GPIL ’s customers are B2B across all product lines — domestic buyers of iron ore pellets, sponge iron, steel billets, 
MS rounds, HB wire, galvanized fabricated products, rolled structural products and ferro alloys. Product quality and 
delivery reliability are the primary value drivers in these relationships.
FY26 SALES PERFORMANCE
FY26 consolidated net sales were `5,381 crore, flattish year-on-year (FY25: `5,376 crore). Volume growth in 
pellets (+15% YoY), sponge iron (+50% YoY), and galvanized fabricated products (+10% YoY) offset softer per-unit 
realisations across most product lines — a market-side pricing dynamic, not a customer relationship deterioration. 
EBITDA margin held at 23% (FY25: 22%), supported by captive ore and power cost advantages. Q4FY26 was 
particularly strong: revenue grew 41% QoQ and 10% YoY, with EBITDA up 91% QoQ and 38% YoY, as the new pellet 
plant ramped to 78% capacity utilisation.
LOCAL PROCUREMENT AND SUPPLIER DEVELOPMENT
GPIL ’s CSR implementation model channels significant execution expenditure through local contractors, Gram 
Panchayats, and village-level service providers. CC road construction, plantation maintenance, community building 
work and local health service operations in FY26 were all executed through local and regional agencies — 
including direct Gram Panchayat engagement at Kachhe, Dafaipara, and Siltara.
The tailoring and stitching centres for rural women — at Dharsiwa (through Waste to Best Welfare Society and 
Usha International) and Kachhe village (directly) — extend procurement relationships into livelihood creation for 
women in the operational communities.Source: GPIL Q4 & FY26 Investor Presentation, May 2026. Realisation is ex-plant, excluding export freight.
PRODUCT QUALITY AND CUSTOMER VALUE
QUALITY POSITIONING
GPIL ’s ~65% Fe magnetite ore enables pellet quality that 
commands a premium of `1,000–1,500/ton over market-priced 
pellets. The new 2 MnT pellet plant — the first in India to deploy 
natural gas-based Grate-Kiln technology — consolidates this 
quality position. ISO 9001:2015 (Quality Management System) 
certification provides the framework for product and process 
quality management across the integrated plant.
Ferro alloy realisations grew 5% YoY to `75,831/ton and galvanized 
fabricated product realisations grew 7% YoY to `77,139/ton — 
reflecting the value attached to finished and specialty products 
versus commodity intermediates.Pellet Premium — Quality to Price

NATURAL CAPITAL
NATURE AT THE 
FOUNDATION OF GROWTH 
Environmental stewardship remains integral to the Company’s long-term sustainability 
and growth strategy. Recognising the importance of responsible resource utilisation, 
the Company focuses on six key pillars—climate and emissions management, renewable 
energy, responsible mining, circular economy practices, water conservation, and 
biodiversity enhancement. Guided by measurable targets and robust governance 
frameworks, these efforts support its commitment to achieving Net Zero Carbon 
Emissions by 2050.The Company’s overarching environmental goal is to achieve Net Zero Carbon Emissions by 2050. This is not a 
distant aspiration — it is a structured, phased and already-in-execution commitment, supported by a published CO2 
intensity reduction trajectory, third-party assured carbon reporting and a portfolio of technology investments that 
collectively de-carbonise each stage of the steel value chain.
Memberships supporting the goal:
India Green Steel Coalition (member); Indian Hydrogen Alliance (member); Ministry of Steel and IIMT Consortium 
for hydrogen utilisation in steel production; CARE Edge ESG Leader (score: 76.6, FY 2024-25, above industry 
median of 71.6).Our Environmental Goal — 
Net Zero Carbon Emissions by 2050
CO2 intensity independently assured by SGS under WSA and CBAM methodologies. E = Estimated. Net Zero target: 
2050.

The Company has maintained a consistent downward trajectory in CO2 intensity since FY 2020-21, declining from 
2.89 tCO2 per tonne to 2.35 tCO2 per tonne on WSA basis in FY 2025-26. This is independently assured by SGS 
under both WSA and CBAM methodologies.
SOLAR EXPANSION PROGRAMME
 25 MW solar plant commissioned 19 May 2026 (capex `72 Crores); grid synchronised and operational
 100 MW solar plant: construction and module installation in progress; commissioning target July 2026 
 (capex `107 Crores incurred to date)
 250 MW solar plant: partial land in possession; government allotment expected June 2026; power evacuation 
 line under construction; commissioning aligned to CRM and ISP timelines
 45 MWh Battery Energy Storage System for captive solar: equipment dispatched; commissioning targeted 
 August 2026
BIOMASS CO-FIRING — AL TERNATIVE FUEL DECARBONISATION
 Biomass pellets (SALDOC — Sal Seed Deoiled Cake) successfully utilised up to 15% as feed mix in the 
 100 TPH biomass power plant, replacing coal
 Successful trial of SALDOC pellets in the pellet plant pilot burner completed; plant-scale burner manufacturing 
 in progress
 Further replacement of coal in the AFBC boiler with biomass pellets is being evaluated, delivering additional 
 decarbonisation
 Biomass co-firing uses agricultural residue as fuel — a circular economy approach that avoids both fossil fuel 
 consumption and agricultural waste disposal
NATURAL GAS FUEL SWITCH — NEW 2 MNT PELLET PLANT
 India’s first natural gas-based Grate-Kiln pellet plant commissioned December 2025: fuel is 100% natural gas 
 (replacing coal gas), under a 7-year MoU with GAIL India Limited
 64% reduction in CO2 per unit of fuel versus coal gas; total CO2 saving approximately 2 Lakh tonnes per annum
 Natural gas combustion produces no particulate matter or SO2 from fuel, reducing local air quality impact 
 at the plant site
 Saved approximately `100 Crores in project capex by adopting natural gas over coal gasification, while 
 achieving a superior environmental outcomeFIVE COMPLETED DECARBONISATION PROJECTS (FY 2025-26)Climate and Carbon Management
