Mining is the foundation of the Company’s integrated model, and preserving the ore body, surrounding land and 
local ecosystem is a core responsibility. In FY 2025-26, the Company made a decisive shift towards electric vehicle 
(EV) operation in its mining fleet, fundamentally changing the environmental and cost profile of ore extraction.
The Company approaches industrial waste as a resource. Steel and mining operations generate iron ore tailings, 
slag, fly ash and process gases — all being converted into useful outputs through circular economy initiatives.The Company minimises freshwater withdrawal through recycling, reuse and closed-loop water systems. Beyond its 
own operations, the Company has invested in community drinking water access through its CSR programme.
The Company actively maintains and expands green cover across plant sites, mine areas and surrounding 
communities. Plantation drives are conducted annually and cumulative sapling count is tracked as a biodiversity 
metric.
Plantation conducted at Siltara plant, Ari Dongri Mine, Boria Tibu Mine and surrounding community areas.IRON ORE TAILINGS UTILISATION — COLLABORATION WITH NIT RAIPUR 
 Paver block production: M30 grade concrete tested with iron ore tailings replacing river sand at 50% ratio; 
 NIT Raipur confirmed 50% sand replacement achieves target compressive strength of 39.10 N/mm² 
 (against target 38.25 N/mm²)
 Ceramic tile production: mix tested using iron ore tailings (40%), clay (40 to 55%) and fluxing material 
 (5 to 20%); four combinations validated
 Impact: eliminates river sand procurement cost (`350 to `400 per tonne) and tailings impoundment cost; 
 river sand conservation protects aquatic ecosystems
 Research ongoing: exploring further applications for tailings in construction materials, paving and ceramicsOPERATIONAL WATER MANAGEMENT 
 Closed-loop water systems: process cooling water recycled and reused, minimising freshwater withdrawal per 
 tonne of output
 Effluent treatment across the Siltara complex; treated effluent recycled for dust suppression, green belt 
 maintenance and horticulture
 Zero Liquid Discharge (ZLD) compliance maintained across applicable units; no untreated industrial effluent  
 discharged into natural water bodies
 Water conservation reviewed under ISO 14001:2015 Environmental Management System and ISO 50001:2018 
 Energy Management System certifications
COMMUNITY WATER ACCESS — CSR
 `237.03 Lakhs allocated to environment and water conservation under CSR in FY 2025-26
 RO water purification systems installed in 100 government schools in Korba: providing safe drinking water to 
 5,463 students and staff (project cost: `1 Crore)
 Drinking water and sanitation is a dedicated CSR vertical, reflecting the Company’s commitment to community 
 water securityWASTE HEAT RECOVERY — CONVERTING PROCESS WASTE TO ENERGY 
 49 MW installed WHR capacity: waste gases from pellet cooler and ferro alloy operations converted 
 to electricity
 7 MW additional WHR CTO received in FY 2025-26; Stages 2 and 3 under commissioning
 Gasifier: FY 2025-26 output of 46,580 Lakh Nm³ was ever-highest annual record at 104.5% of target,  
 maximising energy value from each unit of coal

GOVERNANCE
Responsible stewardship of natural resources remains central for GPIL growth strategy. 
Through focused efforts in emissions reduction, renewable energy, responsible mining, 
circularity, water conservation and biodiversity enhancement, the Company continues to 
strengthen its environmental performance while advancing its commitment to achieve 
Net Zero Carbon Emissions by 2050.Board of Directors
The Board of Godawari Power and Ispat Limited comprises 12 Directors as at 31 March, 2026, representing a 
balanced mix of founding promoter experience, executive management depth and independent professional 
expertise. Six of the twelve Directors are Independent Directors, including two Independent Woman Directors, 
ensuring diverse perspectives in Board deliberations. The Board brings together expertise spanning electronics 
and electrical engineering, finance and chartered accountancy, solar power, international business, capital markets, 
manufacturing operations and corporate governance.

MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION AND ANALYSISANNEXURE 07 TO THE BOARD’S REPORT 
GLOBAL ECONOMY
The global economy navigated a period of heightened 
uncertainty during 2025 and 2026, shaped by the outbreak 
of conflict in the Middle East, persistent geopolitical 
fragmentation, and the continued overhang of trade 
policy disruptions. The IMF’s April 2026 World Economic 
Outlook projected global growth at 3.1% for calendar 
year 2026 and 3.2% for 2027 — a deceleration from the 
3.3% recorded in 2024, with downside risks assessed as 
dominant. Advanced economies — the United States, 
Eurozone, and United Kingdom — are each expected to 
expand at or below 1.5% in 2026, constrained by elevated 
borrowing costs, weak manufacturing recovery, and 
energy market disruptions emanating from the Middle 
East conflict.
Despite softening global trade, the re-emergence of 
selective protectionist measures — most notably the US 
reimposition of steel tariffs to 50% under Section 232 
from April 2026 — and the full operationalisation of the 
EU’s Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM) from 
January 2026 are reshaping global industrial trade flows. 
While these measures create near-term uncertainty for 
export-oriented producers and risk diverting surplus steel 
to alternative markets, the broader industrial outlook 
remains selectively constructive. Global investment 
in infrastructure, energy transition, and urbanisation 
continues to underpin steel demand fundamentals, 
particularly in emerging market and developing economies 
which are projected to grow at above 4% in 2026. India, 
as one of the world’s fastest-growing major economies, 
remains a primary engine of this demand.
The silver lining, however, is that macroeconomic stability 
combined with investment-led growth would prove 
conducive for industrial development and construction 
activities across emerging markets, sustaining future 
steel consumption trends. The OECD projects global GDP 
growth at 2.9% for 2026 before a modest recovery to 3.0% 
in 2027, acknowledging that strong technology investment 
and partial easing of trade tensions provide some upside 
offset against geopolitical headwinds.
https://www.imf.org/-/media/files/publications/weo/202 6/april/english/
ch1.pdf  
https://www.oecd.org/en/publications/oecd-economic-outlook-interim-
report-march-2026_d4623013-en.html
INDIAN ECONOMY
Despite prevailing global uncertainties, India remained one 
of the fastest-growing major economies during FY2025-
26, demonstrating exceptional resilience underpinned by 
robust domestic consumption, accelerating infrastructure 
investment, and a stable macroeconomic policy 
framework. India’s real GDP grew at 7.6% for full-year 
FY2025-26 (revised estimate, April 2026), well above the global average and among the highest of major economies 
worldwide.
India’s inflation trajectory moderated significantly through 
2026, creating a favourable backdrop for sustained 
economic activity and industrial investment. Retail 
inflation (CPI) declined to 3.4% in March 2026 — well 
within the RBI’s 2%–6% tolerance band — while the RBI 
reduced the repo rate to 5.25% through phased cuts. This 
monetary accommodation supported credit expansion 
and investment in capital-intensive sectors.
The Union Budget FY2025-26 allocated ₹11.21 Lakh Crore 
in capital expenditure — a structural commitment to long-
term asset creation. This allocation prioritizes railways, 
highways, urban infrastructure, housing, and logistics — 
sectors that directly drive steel and iron ore consumption. 
The PM Gati Shakti initiative integrated aviation, railways, 
roads, and waterways to enhance connectivity. The PLI 
(Production-Linked Incentive) schemes and manufacturing 
sector momentum further underpin sustained domestic 
steel demand.
The government maintained disciplined fiscal consolidation 
with the fiscal deficit target at 4.3% of GDP for FY2026-
27, while sustaining growth-supportive capital spending. 
This policy mix — monetary accommodation coupled with 
capital-intensive public investment — creates a structurally 
favourable environment for industrial expansion and 
private sector participation in infrastructure projects.
https://www.indiabonds.com/bonduni/news/april-2026-rbi-monetary-
policy-highlights/,https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.

GLOBAL STEEL SCENARIO
Global crude steel production reached approximately 
1,883 MT in 2024. The World Steel Association’s Short 
Range Outlook (SRO) for April 2026 projects global steel 
demand at 1,724.0 MT in 2026 (+0.3% YoY) — a marginal 
recovery from prior-year contraction — before a more 
meaningful rebound to 1,762.0 MT in 2027 (+2.2% YoY).
Among major economies, India is projected to be the 
strongest incremental demand contributor at +7.4% in 
2026 and accelerating to +9.2% in 2027, while China’s 
demand is expected to decline –1.5% YoY in 2026, following 
a –7.1% contraction in 2025. Demand in developing 
nations (excluding China) is projected to grow at 2.5% in 
2026 due to conflict-driven declines in the Middle East, 
with a stronger rebound to 5.1% in 2027, primarily driven 
by India’s continued expansion and recovery in other 
emerging markets.https://worldsteel.org/media/press-releases/2026/worldsteel-short-range-
outlook-april-2026/ ,
https://www.tribuneindia.com/news/construction/indias-crude-steel-output-
rose-10-7-yoy-to-168-4-mt-in-fy-2025-26-ministry-of-steel ,

By Application3China remains the world’s largest crude steel producer 
however, production declined to 960.8 MT in 2025, 
down 4.4% year-on-year from 2024. India ranks second, 
producing 164.9 MT, reflecting the country’s growing 
industrial capacity with 10.4% year-on-year growth. Japan 
follows with approximately 80.7 MT, while the United 
States and Russia occupy the fourth and fifth positions 
with approximately 82 MT and 67.8 MT, respectively.
Global steel overcapacity remains the industry’s most 
intractable challenge. Total excess steelmaking capacity 
reached 640 MT in 2025 and is projected to rise to 
approximately 721 MT by 2027. China’s steel-related 
exports — encompassing finished and semi-finished 
products including billets and pig iron — reached 
approximately 131 MT in 2025, with its share of global 
steel exports rising from 19% in 2019 to 41% in 2025. 
This unprecedented surge in Chinese export volumes is 
intensifying anti-dumping investigations, trade protection 
measures, and price suppression in emerging economies 
across Asia and Southeast Asia.
Steel prices diverged sharply across regions in 2025-26, 
with hot-rolled coil prices in the United States running 
more than twice Chinese benchmarks, while EU prices 
were approximately 50% above Chinese levels — reflecting 
the growing fragmentation of the global steel market driven by protectionist trade policies.
With the reimposition of 50% steel tariffs by the US under 
Section 232 from April 2026, surplus steel from protected 
markets may divert toward alternative destinations — 
particularly Asia and Southeast Asia — heightening 
competition and increasing the risk of oversupply and 
price suppression in the region. Additionally, retaliatory 
trade measures and elevated raw material costs may 
weigh on profitability across the steel value chain. Despite 
these headwinds, strong underlying demand from sectors 
like construction, automotive, renewable energy, and 
manufacturing — especially in Asia and the Middle East — 
is expected to sustain global steel demand fundamentally 
intact over the medium term.
The global steel sector is set to thrive through investment 
in efficiency improvements, technology innovation, and a 
robust focus on sustainability. Technologies such as Carbon 
Capture and Storage (CCS) and green steel produced using 
renewable energy are increasingly becoming imperatives. 
The global EAF (Electric Arc Furnace) steelmaking market 
is projected to grow at approximately 12.6% CAGR over 
2026–2034, underpinning long-term demand for DRI-
grade pellets and low-carbon steelmaking inputs — 
positioning integrated Indian producers with pellet 
operations competitively for the decade ahead.

INDIAN IRON ORE AND IRON ORE PELLETS
Industry Overview
India's iron ore and pellet industry remained supported by strong domestic steel demand in FY2025–26. 
India produced 168.4 MT of crude steel, while finished steel consumption rose to 163.7 MT, reflecting 
sustained demand from infrastructure, construction and manufacturing. Continued government capital 
expenditure of ₹11.21 lakh crore in FY2025–26 further supported demand visibility for steelmaking raw 
materials.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressNoteDetails.aspx?ModuleId=3&NoteId=158442&reg=48&lang=2
Market Dynamics
The market remained structurally favourable for integrated producers, as domestic steel consumption 
continued to grow and policy focus remained aligned with infrastructure creation, manufacturing 
expansion and reducing import dependency. This environment reinforced the strategic relevance of 
captive mining and pelletisation-led integration, with integrated players continuing to hold a structural 
advantage over those reliant on merchant ore purchases.
Production & Consumption
India remained among the world's leading iron ore producers, with national output estimated at 
approximately 289.19 million tonnes in FY2025–26, a decrease of about 1.56% over the previous 
year(as per the Ministry of Mines Annual Report 2025–26). Production remained concentrated in a small 
number of established mining states, with Odisha accounting for 50.28% of national output, followed by 
Chhattisgarh (17.08%) and Karnataka (16.55%). Iron ore continued to be the single largest contributor 
to metallic mineral value, accounting for ₹1,09,623 crore, or 69.64% of the estimated value of metallic 
minerals production in FY2025–26, out of a total metallic minerals value of ₹1,57,415 crore. India 
remained fully self-sufficient in iron ore, with domestic supply exceeding apparent consumption.
Within this national context, iron ore and pellet demand remained closely linked to India's expanding steel 
output. GPIL recorded its highest-ever production during FY2025–26, with iron ore mining increasing to 
27.54 lakh MT from 23.42 lakh MT in FY2024–25. Pellet production also rose to 28.56 lakh MT from 24.49 
lakh MT, supported by higher operating scale and improved integration across the steel value chain.
https://mines.gov.in/admin/download/69945dabea1821771330987.pdf , https://www.livemint.com/market/multibagger-mining-stock-
godawari-power-and-ispat-share-price-jumps-after-reporting-its-highest-ever-production-in-fy26-11775035520400.html
Pricing Trend
The financial performance of India's iron ore and pellet producers in FY2025–26 reflected resilient value 
realisation despite a modest decline in mined volumes. Iron ore remained the dominant contributor 
to metallic mineral value in the country, accounting for ₹1,09,623 crore, or 69.64% of the estimated 
₹1,57,415 crore value of metallic minerals production during the year — itself an increase of approximately 
13.66% over the previous year's metallic minerals value. This divergence between a slight volume decline 
and continued value growth points to firmer average realisations through FY2025–26. Operating costs 
across the sector were shaped by currency movements, regulatory provisioning and freight expenses, 
with integrated players holding captive ore linkages better placed to preserve margins through scale and 
cost efficiency.
https://mines.gov.in/admin/download/69945dabea1821771330987.pdf
INDIAN FERRO ALLOYS MARKET OVERVIEW
Market Overview
The Indian ferro alloys industry continues to benefit from 
the growth of the domestic steel sector, rising infrastructure 
investments and increasing demand for value-added steel 
products. Ferro alloys are critical inputs in steelmaking, 
enhancing strength, hardness, corrosion resistance and 
overall product quality. India produced 168.4 MT of crude 
steel in FY2025-26, registering growth of 10.7% over 
FY2024-25, while finished steel consumption increased 
to 164 MT during the year. Supported by sustained steel 
demand, expanding steelmaking capacity and growing 
downstream applications, the ferro alloys industry remains 
well positioned for long-term growth.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2250022&reg=48&lang=2
Production & Consumption
Production
Capacity & Output
India's ferro alloys industry has an installed production 
capacity of around 5.15 million tonnes per annum and total 
transformer capacity exceeding 2,900 MVA. Total crude 
steelmaking capacity reached approximately 220 MTPA 
in FY2025-26, supporting demand for ferro alloys across 
carbon steel, stainless steel and alloy steel applications.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePageaspx?PRID=2250022&reg=48&lang=2
Product Mix
The industry primarily produces ferro chrome, ferro 
manganese, silico manganese, ferro silicon and other 
specialty alloys. Ferro chrome remains a key product 
category owing to its extensive usage in stainless steel 
manufacturing, while manganese alloys support carbon 
steel and alloy steel production.Consumption
Capacity & Output
Ferro alloy consumption remains closely linked to steel 
production and finished steel demand. Finished steel 
consumption increased from 150.23 MT in FY2024-25 
to 164 MT in FY2025-26, reflecting continued domestic 
demand across infrastructure, construction and 
manufacturing sectors.
End – User Industries
The major consumers of ferro alloys include steel, 
stainless steel, construction, automotive, engineering and 
infrastructure sectors. Rising investments in infrastructure, 
transportation and manufacturing are expected to support 
long-term demand for ferro alloys.
https://www.ibef.org/industry/steel
Opportunities & Threats
Major Opportunities in the Ferro Alloy Sector 
The Indian ferro alloys industry is expected to benefit from 
continued growth in domestic steel production, expanding 
infrastructure investments and increasing demand for 
specialty steel products. Rising steel consumption and 
ongoing capacity expansion across the steel sector are 
expected to create sustained growth opportunities for 
ferro alloy producers.
Major Threats in the Ferro Alloy Sector
The industry remains exposed to volatility in raw material 
prices, energy costs and global steel demand. Fluctuations 
in manganese ore and chrome ore prices, changing trade 
dynamics and the power-intensive nature of ferro alloy 
production may impact profitability and competitiveness.
https://www.business-standard.com/industry/news/global-trade-tensions-a-
threat-for-domestic-ferro-alloys-sector-ifapa-125090900309_1.html

SECTOR WISE GROWTH OPPORTUNITIES FOR INDIAN STEEL INDUSTRY
Automotive
India's automotive sector continued to build momentum during 2025 and 2026 supported by 
robust domestic demand, rising exports and accelerating electric vehicle adoption. The Passenger 
Vehicle segment recorded its highest-ever annual sales of 46.43 lakh units during the year, reflecting 
sustained consumer demand and expanding mobility needs. The growing adoption of electric 
vehicles is expected to increase the use of high-strength and specialised steel grades across battery 
enclosures, structural components and chassis systems, supporting long-term demand across the 
automotive value chain.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2222521&reg=48&lang=2
https://www.siam.in/news-&-updates/press-releases/auto-industry-performance-of-q4-jan--march-2026-fy-2025-26/605
Capital Goods
India's capital goods sector maintained a strong growth trajectory during 2025 and 2026 
supported by the Production Linked Incentive (PLI) Scheme and rising public investment. Public 
capital expenditure was increased to ₹12.2 lakh crore in the FY2026-27 Union Budget, further 
strengthening the investment cycle. This continued momentum is expected to create significant 
opportunities for steel consumption across machinery manufacturing and industrial equipment.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2230621&reg=48&lang=2
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2221455&reg=48&lang=2
Construction & Infrastructure
India's infrastructure sector continued to witness substantial investment during 2025 and 2026 
supported by ongoing development of highways, logistics corridors and public infrastructure. The 
National Highway network expanded to 1,46,560 km, representing a 61% increase since 2014. 
Continued investments in transportation and connectivity infrastructure are expected to drive 
sustained demand for structural steel, rebars, plates and other construction-grade steel products.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2209837&reg=48&lang=2
Railways
Indian Railways continued its modernisation and capacity expansion initiatives during 2025 and 
2026 achieving record freight loading of 1,670 MnT and passenger traffic of 741 crore. Continued 
investments in dedicated freight corridors, network upgrades, station redevelopment and rolling 
stock manufacturing are expected to support sustained demand for rails, structural steel, wagons 
and associated infrastructure.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2248962&reg=48&lang=2
Telecom
India's telecom sector continued to expand rapidly during 2025 and 2026 with 5G services now 
available across 99.9% of districts and more than five lakh 5G base stations deployed nationwide. 
Ongoing network densification, fibre expansion and digital infrastructure investments are expected 
to generate sustained demand for steel-intensive telecom towers and supporting infrastructure.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2206477&reg=48&lang=2
Power
India recorded a landmark year in renewable energy development during 2025 and 2026 with 
non-fossil fuel power capacity reaching 283.46 GW, including 150.26 GW of installed solar capacity. 
Continued investments in renewable energy generation, transmission infrastructure and grid 
expansion are expected to generate sustained demand for structural steel, transmission towers, 
substations and related engineering products.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2250039&reg=48&lang=2
Real Estate & Housing 
India's residential real estate sector continued to demonstrate resilience during 2025 and 2026 
supported by urbanisation, improving housing demand and continued government support for 
affordable housing. The RBI's All-India House Price Index increased by 4.2% year-on-year in Q4 
FY2025-26, reflecting sustained momentum across key housing markets. Continued progress 
under PMAY-U 2.0 and rising urban housing requirements are expected to support construction 
activity and drive demand for rebars, structural steel and other construction materials.
https://www.rbi.org.in/scripts/BS_PressReleaseDisplay.aspx?prid=62833

GOVERNMENT LED INITIATIVES 
The Production Linked Incentive (PLI) Scheme for Specialty Steel continued to strengthen domestic 
manufacturing capabilities by promoting investments in value-added steel products and reducing import 
dependence. During the year, the Ministry of Steel signed MoUs for 85 projects across 55 companies 
under the scheme, involving committed investments of ₹11,887 crore and committed capacity addition 
of 8.7 MnT, further supporting the development of a globally competitive specialty steel sector in India.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2225496&lang=1&reg=1Production Linked Incentive(PLI) Scheme for Specialty Steel
The DMI&SP Policy continued to promote domestic value addition by providing preference to domestically 
manufactured iron and steel products in government procurement. During the year, the policy framework 
was further strengthened through revised procurement provisions aimed at enhancing domestic steel 
manufacturing capabilities, encouraging indigenous technology participation, and supporting self-
reliance in steelmaking technologies across public sector and infrastructure projects.
https://www.pib.gov.in/PressReleasePage.aspx?PRID=2150201&reg=48&lang=2
https://sims.steel.gov.in/DMISP/About.aspxDMI&SP Policy
The QCO framework continued to strengthen quality assurance, consumer safety and industry 
competitiveness by mandating compliance with Bureau of Indian Standards (BIS) specifications. As of 
December 2025, 143 Quality Control Orders covering 723 steel products had been notified, supporting 
the availability of quality-compliant steel products across the domestic market.
https://static.pib.gov.in/WriteReadData/specificdocs/documents/2026/may/doc202655864101.pdfThe Steel Quality Control Order (QCO)

Global Steel Overcapacity
Global steel overcapacity continues to pose a significant 
challenge to the steel industry. According to the OECD, 
global excess steelmaking capacity increased to 640 MnT 
in 2025 and is projected to reach 745 MnT by 2028, despite 
relatively modest growth in steel demand. Persistent 
oversupply may intensify competition, exert downward 
pressure on steel prices and impact industry profitability.
https://www.oecd.org/content/dam/oecd/en/publications/
reports/2026/06/oecd-steel-outlook-2026_a79fb861/99ab9b0c-en.pdf
Trade Protectionism & Tariff Uncertainty
The global steel industry continues to face increasing trade 
restrictions, anti-dumping measures and tariff actions 
across major economies. Rising protectionist policies 
may disrupt international trade flows, alter competitive 
dynamics and create uncertainty for steel exporters. Such 
developments could impact market access and influence 
global steel pricing trends.
https://www.oecd.org/en/about/news/press-releases/2026/06/steel-excess-
capacity-continues-to-weigh-on-global-markets-with-subsidies-increasingly-
undermining-fair-competition.html
Raw Material & Supply Chain Risks
Steel manufacturing remains exposed to fluctuations in 
the prices and availability of critical raw materials such as 
coking coal, metallurgical coke, ferro alloys and energy 
inputs. Ongoing geopolitical tensions, logistics disruptions 
and changes in global trade policies may impact the 
availability and transportation of raw materials, leading to 
procurement challenges, cost escalation and pressure on 
operating margins across the steel value chain.
Energy Transition & Decarbonisation Costs
Increasing environmental regulations and global 
decarbonisation commitments are accelerating the transition towards low-carbon steelmaking technologies. 
Compliance with evolving sustainability standards and 
investments in cleaner production processes may 
require substantial capital expenditure and operational 
adjustments, potentially increasing production costs.
Demand Cyclicality
Steel demand remains closely linked to activity levels across 
construction, infrastructure, automotive, engineering 
and manufacturing sectors. Any slowdown in economic 
growth, investment activity or industrial production may 
adversely affect steel consumption and industry growth 
prospects. Recent revisions to global steel demand 
forecasts highlight the industry's continued sensitivity to 
geopolitical developments and economic conditions.
Review of Operating & Financial Performance
During the year in review, 2025-26, GPIL has maintained 
a stable & satisfactory performance despite facing 
challenges from lower realisations. The consolidated 
revenue figure for 2025-26 is ₹ 5,380.65 crore as compared 
to ₹ 5,375.73 Crore in the previous year, marginally up 
by 0.09%. The company’s consolidated EBITDA totalled 
₹ 1,253 Crore as compared to ₹ 1,194 crores during 
previous year. The company could maintain the EBITDA 
level at 25% as against 22% in the previous year due to 
enhanced plant efficiency together with cost optimisation. 
Net Profit after tax during the year reached Rs. 802 crores 
as the company maintained stable cash flows together 
with disciplined capital allocation. Strategic investments 
between backward integration projects and renewable 
energy capacity expansion advanced both sustainable 
long-term value and profitability.

The Company’s captive iron ore mines, with an estimated reserve of 165 million tonnes and a mine life exceeding 35 
years, continue to provide a strong foundation for long-term resource security. The current mining capacity has been 
enhanced to 6.7 million tonnes in 2026. Backed by a healthy net cash position and a strategically structured capex 
plan, GPIL is well-positioned to execute this expansion plans, ensuring sustained supply chain integration and value 
accretion. During the year under review the Iron Ore mining achieved a year-on-year growth of 18%. Iron Ore Mining
The Company manufactures high grade pellets and has a strong growth plan of expanding its pellet plant capacity 
to 4.7 million tonne in 2025 and 2026 . Due to the superior quality of pellets characterized by low alumina and 
low phosphorus content, the company continues to command a pricing premium in the market. The pellet plant 
operations registered a growth of 17% during the year at a healthy capacity utilisation of 95%. Iron Ore Pellets
During the year the company has received final approval from the Chhattisgarh Environment Conservation Board 
(CECB) for the “Consent to Operate” for its enhanced sponge iron capacity, which has been increased from 0.594 
million MTPA to 0.65 million MTPA. This capacity expansion is expected to further strengthen operational throughput 
in future performance. The company has achieved 100% of its production guidance for 2025–26, reflecting strong 
execution and plant efficiency.Sponge Iron
There is marginal change in the production of Steel Billets, HB Wires, MS Rounds, Galvanized Fabricated Products on 
YoY. Moreover, the company has restarted its rolled structural products during the year and achieved a production of 
90095 MTs as compared to 21499 MTs in the previous year.Finished Steel & Rolled Products

change in the ferro alloys plant of the subsidiary company namely Hira Ferro Alloys Limited. Ferro Alloys
GPIL is actively advancing toward its Net Zero Carbon Emission target by 2050 through increased reliance on 
renewable energy sources such as biomass, waste heat recovery (WHRB), solar, and wind power. The Company has 
developed a robust renewable and clean energy portfolio comprising 190 MW of solar power, 28.5 MW of biomass-
based power, 49 MW from Waste Heat Recovery Boilers (WHRB), and 1.5 MW of wind power. These initiatives not 
only support sustainable operations but also contribute meaningfully to lowering overall emissions. As part of this 
sustainability-driven approach, the company is expanding solar power capacity by additional 100 MW solar power 
project which will support the additional capacity of its upcoming 2 MTPA pellet plant and additional 50,000 MTs Steel 
Billets Capacity and crushing & beneficiation plant at Aridongri mines. In addition to this, the company is also setting 
up another solar power plant of 250 MW capacity for captive use at CRM Project and new Integrated Steel Plant.

12) MATERIAL DEVELOPMENTS IN HUMAN RESOURCE/INDUSTRIAL RELATION
 Maintaining high standard in occupational safety, health & environment is of paramount importance and GPIL believes 
that a secure and healthy workplace is crucial for employee satisfaction, productivity, and overall organisational 
success. The Company implements stringent safety protocols, ongoing training programs, and comprehensive policies, 
including incident reporting and mandatory PPE usage, to prevent accidents and maintain a safe working environment. 
GPIL employs a structured approach to hazard identification, utilising comprehensive risk assessments and job safety 
analyses. Identified risks are prioritised based on severity and likelihood, facilitating the timely implementation of 
appropriate mitigation strategies. Incident and near-miss data are captured and analysed through advanced tracking 
systems, ensuring corrective action and proactive preventative measures. Furthermore, a dedicated incident reporting 
system promotes a culture of safety by encouraging timely employee reporting of hazards and unsafe conditions.
13) RISKS & CONCERNS
 GPIL follows a proactive and structured approach to risk management, recognizing that effective risk oversight is 
essential for sustainable growth and long-term value creation. The Company has established a comprehensive risk 
management framework that enables early identification, assessment, mitigation, and monitoring of internal and 
external risks across all levels of operations.
A. Operational Risk
 Equipment failure, unplanned shutdowns or maintenance-related issues at the Company’s mining, pelletisation, sponge 
iron, steelmaking or power generation facilities may adversely affect production continuity, operational efficiency and 
profitability.
 The Company operates six integrated manufacturing units across multiple locations; a disruption in any one unit can 
have cascading effects on downstream production.
 Risk Mitigation:
• Structured preventive maintenance programme and continuous monitoring of critical equipment across all 
manufacturing facilities;
•  Regular maintenance schedules, process improvement initiatives and employee training programmes to enhance 
plant reliability;
• AI-enabled Integrated Control Room (ICR) monitoring plant operations, vehicular movement and safety compliance 
on a 24×7 basis;
• Strategic inventory buffers maintained for critical raw materials and spare parts to minimise disruption impact.
Annual Report 2025-26
108 109
Godawari Power & Ispat LimitedSTATUTORY REPORT
=== page 58 ===
B.Input and Raw Material Risk
Fluctuations in prices and availability of key raw
materials — iron ore, coking coal, coke, ferro alloys,
natural gas and energy inputs — directly impact
production costs, margins and competitiveness. Global 
supply chain disruptions, geopolitical developments,import competition and changes in trade policies may
affect procurement costs and operating margins.
Risk Mitigation:
•Captive iron ore mining with proven reserves
provides 35+ years of supply security and cost
advantage; long term supply MoU with GAIL India
Limited; fuel cost and supply risk substantially
reduced; Beneficiation plant diminishes external
ore procurement.
C.Commodity Price and Market Risk
 Steel, iron ore and pellet realisations are subjectto global commodity price cycles, driven by China
demand trends, global overcapacity and demand
cyclicality across construction, infrastructure,
automotive and manufacturing sectors. Global excess
steelmaking capacity may exert sustained downward
pressure on realisations.
Risk Mitigation:
•  Vertical integration shields the Company from
merchant input cost volatility; pellet and DRI cost
advantages persist regardless of market pricing;
Product mix diversification, Merchant pellet
premium through high-grade 65% Fe product
quality, reducing correlation to commodity pellet
benchmarks; Export flexibility.
D.Pr oject Execution and Commissioning Risk
The Company is executing capital expenditure
programme across the CRM Complex, Integrated
Steel Plant, BESS manufacturing, solar expansion
and beneficiation plant. Delays in project
implementation, regulatory approvals, equipmentdelivery, commissioning schedules or cost overruns
may impact expected financial returns and strategic
timelines. Equipment leads times from globalsuppliers, contractor capacity constraints and multi-
regulatory approval sequences create execution risk
across several concurrent projects.
Risk Mitigation
•Structur ed project execution framework with
regular monitoring and review mechanisms; each
project has a dedicated management team and
Board-level oversight; Phased capex sequencing;E.Financial and Le verage Risk
Lar ge-scale capital expenditure programmes, if not
managed within prudent financial guardrails, may
increase leverage, raise finance costs and constrain
financial flexibility. Marginal deterioration in debt-
equity ratio (0.07x in FY26 vs 0.06x in FY25) reflects
early project borrowings; this ratio is expected to rise
modestly as ISP and BESS ramp up.
Risk Mitigation
•1:1 debt-equity guar drail applied to all major
projects (ISP and BESS); equity component funded
entirely from internal accruals — no equity dilution 
planned;
•Cash flow from operations provides full internal
funding for FY 2026-27 capex without additional
borrowing;
•Net cash position and interest coverage ratio
provide substantial debt headroom;
• Debt capital has been lined up befor e construction 
commencement, reducing refinancing risk.
F.Regulatory and Envir onmental Risk
Mining operations require continuous regulatory
compliance with environmental clearances, Consent toOperate (CTO), CTS and forest clearances. Any delay in
obtaining or renewing approvals may disrupt mining
and production continuity. Increasingly stringent
environmental regulations and global decarbonisation 
commitments may require substantial additional
capital expenditure. EU CBAM (fully operational
from January 2026) imposes a carbon cost on steel
imports based on embedded CO₂ intensity, creating
compliance and cost risk for carbon-intensiveproducers.
Risk Mitigation
•All key regulatory milestones achieved in FY 2025-
26: CTO for Ari Dongri Mine (6 MnT), CTO for DRI
expansion, CTO for HB Wire, CTO for 7 MW WHR
plant;
•C O₂ intensity of 2.50 tCO₂ per tonne of steel
(CBAM basis; FY 2025-26 average; independently
assured by SGS) is already competitive against EU
benchmark steelmakers;
•Five energy efficiency and decarbonisation
projects completed in FY 2025-26; CCU pilot (3
TPD) commissioned with IIT Mumbai; biomass co-
firing trials at 15% mix under way;•Net Zero Carbon Emissions target 2050,
supported by declining CO₂ intensity trajectory:
2.89 (FY21) → 2.35 (FY26) → 2.10 (FY28E) → 0 (FY50).
G.Location and Geogr aphic Concentration Risk
GPIL ’s manufacturing facilities, captive mines and
power generation assets are concentrated primarilyin Chhattisgarh. Adverse developments arising
from regional regulatory changes, infrastructure
disruptions, natural events, labour availability issues
or logistics constraints may affect business operations
and supply chain continuity. Logistics costs (especially
mining transport) are subject to diesel price volatility,
as mining vehicles are predominantly fuel-dependent.
Risk Mitigation
•Integr ated operating model with captive
mining and captive power generation reduces
dependence on external infrastructure at critical
production stages;
•Str ategic inventory management and proactive
logistics planning strengthen operational
resilience against regional disruptions;
•EV fleet transition in mining and manufacturing
operations reduces diesel price exposure;10 EV dumpers, 24 EV loaders and 15 EV
excavators already reducing transport costs by
approximately 75%.
H.T rade Protectionism and Global Macroeconomic
Risk
Rising trade protectionism — including the US
reimposition of 50% steel tariffs under Section 232
from April 2026 and the EU’s CBAM — is reshaping
global steel trade flows and creating uncertainty for
exporters. Surplus steel diverted from protected
markets may intensify competition in India’s domestic
market. IMF projects global growth at 3.1% in 2026,
with advanced economies growing at or below
1.5%. A broader economic slowdown could dampen
infrastructure investment and steel demand.
Risk Mitigation
•Pr edominantly domestic revenue base: the
Company’s revenues are primarily India-driven, providing insulation from direct export 
revenue disruption;
•India ’s steel demand is projected to grow at +7.4%
in 2026 and accelerate to +9.2% in 2027 (WorldSteel Association) — the strongest trajectory
among major economies;
•Go vernment capital expenditure of Rs. 11.21 Lakh
Crore in FY 2025-26 provides a demand floor
independent of private sector cyclicality;
•Export optionality maintained: CBAM-compliant
gas pellets can be directed to export marketsif domestic demand softens, as confirmed by
management in the Q4 FY 2025-26 earnings call.
14. INTERNAL CONTROL SYSTEM AND THEIR
ADEQUACY
The Company implements an internal control system
of appropriate size and scale and level of complexity
of its operations. The scope and authority of Internal
Audit functions reporting to the Chairman of Audit
Committee has been established in the Internal Audit,in order to preserve its objectivity and independence.
The Internal Audit department continuously monitors
and reviews the effectiveness and suitability of itsinternal control system in the Company, its adherence
with the operating system, accounting procedures
and Company policies and subsidiaries. Based on theInternal Auditors’ report, process owners undertake
corrective actions in their respective areas and thereby 
strengthen the control. Significant audit observationsand corrective actions thereon are placed before the
Audit Committee of the Board.
15.CAUTIONARY ST ATEMENT
The above Management Discussion and Analysis
describing the Company’s objectives, projections,
estimates and expectations may be “forward looking
statements” within the meaning of applicable
securities laws and regulations. Actual results could
differ materially from those expressed or implied.
Important factors that could make a difference to
the Company’s operations include external economic
conditions affecting demand/supply influencing
price conditions in the market in which the Company
operates, changes in Government regulations, taxlaws, and other incidental factors.

matters that present a risk or an opportunity to your business, rationale for identifying the same, approach to adapt or

1 Energy 
ManagementRisk The steel industry is highly 
energy-intensive, with emissions 
primarily from fuel consumption 
in operations. As regulatory 
pressure intensifies, there is 
a growing risk of penalties for 
exceeding emission limits and 
increasing compliance costs. 
Additionally, the rising demand 
from investors for sustainable 
practices further compounds 
the need for emission reduction, 
creating both environmental and 
financial risks.To address this risk, GPIL is 
focused on adopting Best 
Available Technologies to 
reduce emissions and improve 
operational efficiency.
Efforts to improve energy 
efficiency include reducing fossil 
fuel use, optimising processes, 
and transitioning towards 
renewable energy. GPIL have 
installed a 142 MW solar plant, 
and around 100 MW are in the 
pipeline.Negative
Opportunity Procurement of renewable 
energy and implementation 
of energy efficiency initiative 
can help in cost savings in 
operations and offsetting 
emissions.NA Positive
2 Water 
ManagementRisk Water is a critical and limited 
natural resource essential for all 
operational processes, and its 
scarcity poses a potential risk to 
future business operations.GPIL mitigates water-related 
risks through a comprehensive 
water management approach 
focused on conservation, 
efficiency, and sustainable 
resource utilization. The 
Company adopts the principles 
of Reduce, Reuse, Recycle, and 
Recover by implementing best 
available technologies across 
its operations. Freshwater 
withdrawal is minimized through 
extensive recycling and reuse 
of treated wastewater within 
plant processes. GPIL has 
also implemented rainwater 
harvesting systems at its plant 
locations to enhance water 
conservation and improve 
water availability. In addition, 
the Company is strengthening 
water recycling practices 
and deploying Zero Liquid 
Discharge (ZLD) technologies 
to reduce wastewater 
discharge. Detailed water 
audits are regularly conducted 
to identify water losses and 
uncover opportunities for 
improving water efficiency and 
conservation across operations.NegativeSr.

3 Waste 
ManagementRisk Waste generation and 
improper disposal can lead 
to environmental pollution, 
regulatory non-compliance, 
increased operational costs, and 
reputational risksThe waste generated from the 
GPIL’s facilities is systematically 
segregated at the source into 
various categories, including 
hazardous and non-hazardous 
waste, to ensure safe handling, 
storage, treatment, and 
disposal. Waste disposal is 
carried out through authorized 
and Pollution Control Board-
approved vendors in accordance 
with applicable environmental 
laws and regulatory 
requirements. We continuously 
monitor its waste management 
practices to ensure compliance, 
minimize environmental impact, 
promote recycling and resource 
recovery wherever feasible, and 
strengthen sustainable waste 
management across operations.Negative
4 Sustainable 
Supply ChainRisk Effective supplier management 
is critical to ensuring 
uninterrupted operations, 
quality assurance, timely 
availability of raw materials, and 
overall supply chain resilience. 
Dependence on multiple 
suppliers across geographies 
exposes the Company to risks 
such as supply disruptions, price 
volatility, quality non-compliance, 
logistical challenges, regulatory 
issues, and ESG-related 
concerns.We adopt a structured supplier 
management approach through 
supplier evaluation, vendor 
qualification, and periodic 
performance assessments 
based on quality, delivery 
and compliance parameters. 
Diversification of the supplier 
base, long-term procurement 
planning, and strategic 
sourcing practices help 
reduce dependency on single 
suppliers and mitigate supply 
chain disruptions. Regular 
engagement with suppliers, 
adherence to procurement 
policies, and monitoring of 
regulatory and ESG compliance 
further strengthen supply 
chain reliability and responsible 
sourcing practices.Negative

supply of materials, labor and 
sharing of regional resources. 
Also, being a community 
benefactor enhances the 
Company’s reputation.NA Positive

3. Describe the processes in place to safely reclaim your products for reusing, recycling and disposing at the 
end	of	life,	for	(a)	Plastics	(including	packaging)	(b)	E-waste	(c)	Hazardous	waste	and(d)other	waste.		
 GPIL does not have a formal product take-back or end-of-life reclamation program, as its products are supplied to 
industrial and commercial customers and are managed by them after sale.  However, GPIL has established procedures 
for the safe management of plastic waste, e-waste, hazardous waste, and other waste streams generated from its 
operations. Waste is segregated, stored, recycled, utilized, or disposed of through authorized agencies in compliance 
with applicable regulations. The Company also promotes the beneficial utilization of process by-products such as fly 
ash, ESP dust, slag, and mill scale to minimize landfill disposal and enhance resource efficiency.

 Yes. EPR are applicable to the GPIL. We have registered ourselves as importer. We implement an EPR plan in line with 
regulatory requirements.

Total 2083 2083 100% 1824 1824 100%10. Health and safety management system:
a. Whether an occupational health and safety management system has been implemented by the entity? (Yes/ No) . 
If yes, the coverage such system?
 Yes. GPIL has implemented an Occupational Health and Safety Management System across all its facilities. 
The system is certified to ISO 45001 and covers all employees, workers, and operational activities. It provides 
a structured framework for hazard identification, risk assessment, incident prevention, legal compliance, and 
continual improvement of workplace health and safety performance. 
b. What are the process ensured to identify work-related hazards and assess risks on a routine and non-routine basis 
by the entity?
 GPIL identifies work-related hazards and assesses risks through a structured process comprising Hazard 
Identification and Risk Assessment (HIRA), workplace inspections, safety audits, incident investigations, and 
management of change reviews for both routine and non-routine activities. Appropriate control measures are 
implemented based on the risk assessment outcomes. The effectiveness of these processes is periodically 
reviewed through internal audits and independent third-party assessments conducted as part of the Company's 
ISO 9001, ISO 14001, ISO 45001, ISO 50001, and ISO 27001 certification audits.
c. Whether you have processes for workers to report the work-related hazards and to remove themselves from such 
risks. (Y/N)
 Yes. GPIL has established processes that enable employees and workers to identify and report work-related 
hazards through supervisors, safety officers, Safety Committees, workplace inspections, and near-miss reporting 
systems. Workers are encouraged to promptly report unsafe conditions and practices, which are investigated and 
addressed through appropriate corrective and preventive actions. The Company also empowers workers to stop 
work and remove themselves from situations that they reasonably believe pose an imminent risk to their health or 
safety, without fear of reprisal.
d. Do the employees/worker of the entity have access to non-occupational medical and healthcare services? (Yes/ No)
 Yes. GPIL provides access to non-occupational medical and healthcare services to its employees and workers 
through company-operated medical facilities, health insurance coverage, periodic health check-ups, and access to 
qualified medical professionals.

1. GPIL is committed to maintaining a safe and healthy workplace through its ISO 45001-certified Occupational 
Health and Safety Management System implemented across all facilities. The Company conducts regular hazard 
identification and risk assessments, workplace inspections, safety audits, and incident investigations to proactively 
identify and mitigate potential risks.
2. All employees and workers undergo comprehensive safety induction training upon joining and receive periodic 
refresher training to strengthen awareness of safety procedures, emergency response requirements, and safe 
work practices. Appropriate Personal Protective Equipment (PPE), safety devices, warning signage, and other 
protective measures are provided based on job-specific risks.
3. GPIL has established emergency preparedness and response plans covering scenarios such as fire, medical 
emergencies, gas leaks, and other potential incidents. Regular mock drills are conducted to ensure preparedness 
and effective response. The Company also provides occupational health services, periodic health check-ups, and 
wellness initiatives to support the physical and mental well-being of employees.
4. Worker participation is encouraged through Safety and Works Committees, near-miss reporting systems, safety 
observations, and open communication channels for reporting hazards and unsafe conditions. Compliance with 
applicable legal requirements and international management system standards is regularly assessed through 
internal reviews and independent third-party audits under ISO 9001, ISO 14001, ISO 45001, ISO 50001, and ISO 
27001 certifications. GPIL remains committed to continual improvement of its health and safety performance 
through employee engagement, technological upgrades, and adoption of industry best practices.

on	significant	 risks/concerns	 arising	from	assessments	 of	health	&	safety	practices	 and	working	 conditions.
 GPIL follows a structured incident investigation and corrective action process to address safety-related incidents and 
identified risks. Any incidents, near misses, unsafe acts, or unsafe conditions are thoroughly investigated to determine 
root causes, and appropriate corrective and preventive actions are implemented. 
 Significant risks and concerns identified through hazard identification and risk assessments, workplace inspections, 
audits, and employee feedback are regularly reviewed by management and Safety Committees. Necessary measures 
are implemented to mitigate risks and improve working conditions. The effectiveness of corrective actions is monitored 
through periodic reviews, inspections, and audits to ensure continual improvement in occupational health and safety 
performance across all facilities.Businesses should respect the interests of and be responsive to all its stakeholdersPRINCIPLE 4
Essential Indicators
1. Describe the processes for identifying key stakeholder groups of the entity.
 GPIL identifies its key stakeholders through a structured stakeholder mapping process that considers the nature of 
engagement with the Company, the stakeholders' influence on business operations, and the potential impacts of the 
Company's activities on them. Inputs from business risk assessments, sustainability priorities, regulatory requirements, 
and ongoing stakeholder interactions are used to determine stakeholder relevance and significance. The stakeholder 
groups are reviewed periodically to ensure alignment with GPIL's operational footprint, strategic objectives, and 
evolving stakeholder expectations.

5. Has the entity implemented a mechanism for Zero Liquid Discharge? if yes, provide details of its coverage   
and implementation.  
 Yes. The Company has implemented Zero Liquid Discharge (ZLD) mechanisms across its operations to minimize 
freshwater consumption and eliminate the discharge of untreated wastewater outside the plant premises. Wastewater 
generated from industrial processes is treated through appropriate effluent treatment systems and is recycled and 
reused within operations wherever feasible. The Company continuously undertakes measures to improve water 
recovery, optimize water consumption, and strengthen wastewater management practices in line with regulatory 
requirements and sustainable water management principles.

8. Does the entity have any project related to reducing Green House Gas emission? If yes, then provide details.  
 Yes, we have implemented multiple projects for reducing greenhouse gas emissions from our operations. The Company 
has significantly increased the share of renewable energy in its overall energy mix to reduce GHG emissions and 
support low-carbon operations. Currently, around 69% of the total electricity consumed is sourced from renewable 
energy, while the remaining share is met through non-renewable sources. In addition, several energy efficiency 
and process optimization initiatives have been implemented across operations to reduce energy consumption and 
improve operational efficiency. Key initiatives include process optimization in the pellet plant to reduce producer gas 
consumption, enhancement of kiln diameter for improved process efficiency, fuel shifting from coal to LPG in the new 
pellet plant, and modification of the 25 MW turbine rotor to reduce specific steam consumption. These initiatives have 
contributed towards lowering energy intensity, optimizing fuel utilization, and reducing the overall carbon footprint of 
operations.

 `Note: Indicate if any independent assessment/evaluation/assurance has been carried out by an external agency? (Y/N) 
If yes, name of the external agency.
 Yes, independent assurance was carried out by TÜV SÜD South Asia Pvt. Ltd.10.	Briefly	 describe	 the	waste	management	 practices	 adopted	 in	your	establishments.	 Describe	 the	strategy	
adopted	 by	your	company	 to	reduce	usage	of	hazardous	 and	toxic	chemicals	 in	your	products	 and	processes	
and the practices adopted to manage such wastes.
 The Company follows a comprehensive waste management approach based on the principles of Reduce, Reuse, 
Recycle, and Reclaim to minimize environmental impact and promote circular economy practices across its operations. 
Continuous efforts are undertaken to optimize resource utilization, reduce waste generation, and maximize recovery 
and reuse of by-products and waste materials.
 Under the Reuse approach, materials such as coal tar, dolochar, and metals are reused internally within the 
manufacturing process wherever feasible. Under Recycle, various waste streams including fly ash, Coal tar, used oil, 
steel scrap, metals, plastic waste, e-waste, battery waste, ETP sludge, and zinc ash are recycled through authorized 
recyclers and approved vendors in compliance with applicable regulations.
 As part of the Reclaim philosophy, ESP dust and iron ore fines are utilized for land reclamation activities to minimize 
landfill disposal and support resource recovery. Construction and demolition waste, along with a limited quantity of fly 
ash, is scientifically disposed of through designated landfill facilities in accordance with regulatory requirements.

Assurance Report
Assurance	statement	on	third-party	verification	of	sustainability	information
To 
The Directors of GODAWARI POWER & ISPAT LIMITED,
Plot No. 428/2, Industrial Area, Phase-1, Siltara, Chhattisgarh, Raipur - 493111 INDIA.
Unique identification no.: 3153164680
TÜV SÜD South Asia Pvt Ltd. (hereinafter TÜV SÜD) has been engaged by, GODAWARI POWER & ISPAT LIMITED  
(hereinafter “Company”) for the period from 01-04-2025 to 31-03-2026. 
The verification was carried out according to the steps and methods described below. 
Scope	of	the	verification
The third-party verification was conducted to obtain independent assurance about whether the Sustainability information 
is prepared in reference to BRSR standard/framework (hereinafter referred as “Reporting Criteria”).
Reporting standard/framework
The disclosures have been prepared by, GODAWARI POWER & ISPAT LIMITED,  in reference to:
MASTER CIRCULAR- SEBI/HO/CFD/PoD2/CIR/P/0155, dated November 11, 2024, corresponding format of ‘BRSR 9 Core 
Attributes’.
The following sustainability indicators’ reporting are included in the scope of the assurance engagement during the 
reporting period Financial Year (FY) 2025-2026 as listed below
Reasonable level of assurance of ‘BRSR 9 Core Attributes’ 
Other than as described in the preceding paragraph, which sets out the scope of our engagement, we did not perform 
assurance procedures on the remaining information included in the BRSR reporting, and accordingly, we do not express a 
conclusion on this information.
It was not part of our engagement to review product- or service-related information, references to external information 
sources, expert opinions and future-related statements in the Report.
Responsibility of the Company
The legal representatives of the Company are responsible for the preparation of the BRSR report in accordance with the 
Reporting Criteria. This responsibility includes in particular the selection and use of appropriate methods for measurement, 
calculation, collection and compilation of information and the making of appropriate assumptions or, where appropriate, 
the making of appropriate estimates. Furthermore, the legal representatives are responsible for necessary internal controls 
to enable the preparation of a BRSR report that is free of material - intentional or unintentional - erroneous information.
Verification	methodology	and	procedures	performed
The verification engagement has been planned and performed in accordance with the verification methodology developed 
by the TÜV SÜD Group which is based on ISAE 3000 assurance engagement standard and ISO 17029.
Level of Assurance
Reasonable Level of assurance for the 9 core attributes of BRSR (Ref: Annexure I of SEBI circular)
The verification was based on a systematic and evidence-based assurance process limited as stated above. The selection 
of assurance procedures is subject to the auditor’s own judgment.- Inquiries of personnel who are responsible for the stakeholder engagement and materiality analysis to understand the 
reporting boundaries
- Evaluation of the design and implementation of the systems and processes for compiling, analysing, and aggregating 
sustainability information as well as for internal controls
- Inquiries of company’s representatives responsible for collecting, preparing and consolidating sustainability information 
and performing internal controls
- Analytical procedures and inspection of sustainability information as reported at group level by all locations
- Assessment of local data collection and management procedures and control mechanisms through a sample survey 
at below selected site as mentioned below:
Sl. No. Company Name Site Address
1 GODAWARI POWER & ISPAT LIMITED Plot No. 428/2, Industrial Area, Phase-1, Siltara, 
Chhattisgarh Raipur – 493111, India
Conclusion
Reasonable level of Assurance- BRSR 9 Core Attributes
On the basis of the assessment procedures carried out & evidence we have collected during 11-05-2026 to 03-07-2026, 
the identified sustainability indicators of 9 Core Attributes (Listed in Annexure I of this statement) of BRSR for FY 2025-2026 
are prepared in all material respect in accordance with the reporting requirements outlined in BRSR Core.
Limitations
The assurance process was subject to the following limitations:
- The subject matter information covered by the engagement are described in the “scope of the engagement”. Assurance 
of further information included in the BRSR reporting was not performed. Accordingly, TÜV SÜD do not express a 
conclusion on this information.
- The assurance scope excluded forward-looking statements, product- or service-related information, external 
information sources and expert opinions.
Use of this Statement
The Company must reproduce the TÜV SÜD statement and possible attachments like Assurance report in full and without 
omissions, changes, or additions.
This statement is by the scope of the engagement solely intended to inform the Company as to the results of the mandated 
assessment. TÜV SÜD has not considered the interest of any other party in the selected sustainability information, this 
assurance report or the conclusions TÜV SÜD has reached. Therefore, nothing in the engagement or this statement 
provides third parties with any rights or claims whatsoever. 
Independence	and	competence	of	the	verifier
TÜV SÜD South Asia Pvt Ltd. is an independent certification and testing organization and member of the international TÜV 
SÜD Group, with accreditations also in the areas of social responsibility and environmental protection. The assurance team 
was assembled based on the knowledge, experience and qualification of the auditors. TÜV SÜD South Asia Pvt Ltd. hereby 
declares that there is no conflict of interest with the Company.

2.REVIEW OF PERFORMANCE:
Your Company’s performance during the year
under review was satisfactory, in the given market
conditions. The Company has reported healthyoperating margins of 28% on standalone operationsand 25% on consolidated operations lead by highervolume of production in Iron Ore Mining, Iron OrePellet, Sponge Iron, despite lower sales realizationsin all products except Ferro Alloys and GalvanizedFabricated Products. Given the above backdrop, thehighlights of standalone & consolidated results aregiven below:
Standalone Operations:
Revenue from operations for the year marginally 
increased by 1.13% to `4713.96 Crores as 
compared to `4661.24 Crores during previous Financial Year .
EBITDA for the year increased by 12.36% to  
` 1370.24 Crores  as compared to  `1219.56 Crores 
during previous Financial Year .
Profit after tax increased by 19.46% to ` 919.43 Crores as compared to ` 769.64 Crores in previous 
Financial Year .
Consolidated Operations:
Revenue from operations excluding other income for the year increased by 0.09% to `5380.65 Crores as compared to ` 5375.73 Crores during 
the previous Financial Year;
EBITDA for the year increased by 4.46% to `1347.21 Crores as compared to ` 1289.69 Crores 
during previous Financial Year .
Profit after tax during the year decreased by 1.38% to `801.74 Crores as compared to ` 812.98 Crores during previous Financial Year .
The detailed comments on the operating and 
financial performance of the Company, during year under review have been given in the Management Discussions & Analysis.

4. SCHEME OF AMALGAMATION:
 During the year under review, the Board of Directors 
in its meeting held on 05th August, 2025 approved the 
Scheme of Amalgamation of Godawari Energy Limited 
(GEL) with Godawari Power and Ispat Limited (GPIL). 
The Hon’ble National Company Law Tribunal (NCLT), 
Cuttack Bench has sanctioned the aforesaid Scheme 
of Amalgamation vide order dated 10th March, 
2026 and a Corrigendum order has been issued by 
Hon’ble NCLT dated on 11th March, 2026 due to minor 
typographical non-material correction in the NCLT 
Order dated 10th March, 2026. Pursuant to the Hon’ble 
NCLT order , Godawari Energy Limited stands merged 
with Godawari Power and Ispat Limited with effect 
from 23rd March, 2026.
5. SHARE CAPITAL:
A. Increase in Authorised Share Capital due to 
Merger:
 Consequent upon coming into effect of the 
above Scheme of Amalgamation, the Authorized 
share Capital of the Godawari Energy Limited 
amounting to `25,00,00,000 has been added to 
the Authorised Share Capital of the Company. 
Accordingly, the Authorized Share Capital of the 
Company has been increased to `99,00,00,000/- 
(Rupees Ninety-Nine Crores only) divided into 
95,80,00,000 Equity Shares of Re.1/- each and 
32,00,000 Preference Shares of `10/-each.
B. Issue of Warrants convertible into Equity:
 During the year under review, the Board of Directors of the Company, at its meeting held 
on 18th September 2025, approved the issue 
of 2,04,08,220 warrants convertible into equity 
shares on a preferential basis to the Promoter 
Group and Non-Promoter Group Investors at a 
price of `245 per warrant convertible into equal 
number of Equity Shares of Re. 1/= each fully paid 
at a premium of `244 per equity share.
 The shareholders of the Company accorded their 
approval for the aforesaid preferential issue in 
their Extraordinary General Meeting held on 15th 
October 2025.Thereafter , the Board of Directors, 
at its meeting held on 14th November 2025, 
approved the allotment of 2,04,08,220 warrants 
on a preferential basis to the eligible allottees, 
in accordance with the applicable provisions 
of the Companies Act, 2013 and Securities and 
Exchange Board of India (ICDR) Regulations 2018.
 None of the Directors except Mr . Dinesh Agrawal 
has been allotted any convertible warrants.

7. EXPANSION/NEW PROJECTS:
A. Cold Rolling Mill (CRM) Project:
 The Company is setting up of 0.7 MTPA Cold 
Rolling Mill (CRM) Project for manufacture of Heavy 
Structural Steel at Village: Sarora, Tehsil: Tilda Dist: 
Raipur , Chhattisgarh at an estimated project cost 
of `900 Crores. The project is registered under 
the ambit of PLI 1.1 and PLI 1.2 incentives.
B. Setting Up of 40_Gwh Battery Energy Storage 
System (Bess) Plant:
 Battery Energy Storage System (BESS) Project 
of 40 GWh in two phases (Phase-I – 20GWh in 
FY26-27 and Phase-II-20GWh in FY28-29) has 
been undertaken by the company’s wholly owned 
subsidiary namely Godawari New Energy Private 
Limited (GNEPL) with an envisage total project 
cost of `1625 Crores including working capital 
margin Money.
 Similarly, the Company is setting up a Battery 
Energy Storage System (BESS) with a capacity 
of 45 MWh at the Company’s solar power plant 
located at Mahurmkala, at an estimated project 
cost of approximately ` 40 Crores.C. Integrated Steel Plant:
 The Company is setting up an Integrated Steel 
Plant with a capacity to manufacture 1.00 
million tons per annum of Iron & Steel finished 
products in the form of heavy & medium section 
structural steel and wire rods to strengthen its 
presence in the steel sector and capitalize on 
the growing demand for structural steel in India. 
The Integrated Steel Plant is proposed to be set 
up at village Sarora, Tehsil Tilda, District Raipur , 
Chhattisgarh, which is 50 KMs away from Raipur , 
Chhattisgarh. The project cost is envisaged at 
`7000 Crores to be funded through the mix of 
debt and equity (internal accruals).
D. Solar Power Capacities:
 The Company is expanding its captive solar power 
capacity from 165 MW to 540 MW i.e. additional 
375 MW to support its iron ore mines, additional 
2 million tons pellet plant, CRM, and 1-million-
ton steel plant operations. Out of 375 MW, 25 
MW solar project has already commenced in 
the month of May, 2026. 100 MW solar project 
construction and module installation activities are 
in progress with completion targeted in Q2FY27. 
250 MW solar project progressing with partial 
land in possession and balance government land 
allotment. Power evacuation line construction is 
underway. Project commissioning is aligned with 
the end-use projects.

Particulars FY26 FY25 % Change
Production Volumes

the Company, its compliance with operating system, 
accounting procedures and policies of the Company 
and its subsidiaries. Based on the report of the Internal 
Auditors, process owners undertake corrective actions 
in their respective areas and thereby strengthen the 
control. Significant Audit observations and corrective 
actions thereon are presented to the Audit Committee 
of the Board.

Limited’s books, paper , minute books, forms, and return

of secretarial audit and as per the explanations given to 
me and the representation made by the management, I 
hereby report that in my opinion, the company has, during 
the audit period covering the financial year ended on 
31st March, 2026 generally complied with the statutory 
provisions listed hereunder and also that company has 
proper Board process and compliance mechanism in place 
to the extent, in the manner and subject to the reporting 
made hereinafter:

and returns filed and other records maintained by the 
Company for the financial year ended on March 31, 2026

by management, the company has generally complied with

Standards, etc. mentioned above. I further report that

Indirect Tax laws by the Company has not been reviewed 
in this Audit since the same has been subject to review 
by Statutory Auditors and other designated Professionals.
I further report that the Board of Directors & the 
Committees of the Board were duly constituted with the 
proper balance of Executive, Non- Executive Directors and 
independent Directors. The Changes in the composition of 
the Board of Directors that took during the period under 
review were carried out in compliance with the provisions 
of the Act.
Adequate notice is given to all Directors to schedule the 
Board Meetings, agenda and detailed notes on agenda 
were sent at least seven days in advance, and a system 
exists for seeking and obtaining further information and 
clarifications on the agenda items before the meeting and 
for meaningful participation at the meeting.
Majority decision is carried through while the dissenting 
members’ views are captured and recorded as part of the 
minutes.
I further report that  as per the explanation given to 
me and the representation made by the management 
and relied upon by me there are adequate systems and 
processes in the company commensurate with the size 
and operations of the company to the monitor and ensure 
compliance with applicable laws, rules, regulations and 
guidelines.

regulations, guidelines, standards, etc. referred to above,

correctness of the contents of the Secretarial records. The verification was done on test basis to ensure that correct 
facts are reflected in secretarial records. I believe that the process and practices, I followed provide a reasonable basis 
for my opinion.
3. I have not verified the correctness and appropriateness of financial records and Books of Accounts of the company.
4. Wherever required, I have obtained the management representative about the compliance of laws, rules, and 
regulations and happening of events etc.
5. The Compliance of the provision of corporate and other applicable laws, rules, regulation, standards is the responsibility 
of management. My examination was limited to the verification of procedure on test basis.
6. The secretarial audit report is neither an assurance as to the future viability of the company nor of the efficacy or 
effectiveness with which the management has conducted the affairs of the company.
7. The List of law applicable to the Company to the secretarial Audit Report is as confirmed by the Management of the 
Company. The secretarial audit report is neither an assurance nor a confirmation that the list is exhaustive.

(A) CONSERVATION OF ENERGY-
(i)The steps taken or impact on conservation of energy:
Steps Taken: 
1) Reduced Specific Electrical energy consumption in SID by process optimization
2) Reduced Specific coal consumption in SID by Process optimization.
3) TG- 5 rotor and internal parts replacement;
4) Reduced Auxiliary power consumption in Power Plant by process optimization
5) Reduced specific electrical energy consumption in Pellet Plant by process optimization;
6) Reduced specific producer gas consumption in Pellet Plant by process optimization;
The impact of above measures:
1) Reduction of Specific Power Consumption from 58.02 Kwh to 51.85 Kwh  ie 6.17Kwh/MT of DRI (4009834 Kwh yearly) 
by Enhancement of Process Parameters to get higher Production of 649989 MT.
2) Reduction of Specific coal consumption from 0.799 to 0.779 by Enhancement of Process Parameters to save 12999.78 
Tons of coal.
3) Increased annual power generation by 1.08 MWh due to SSC reduced from 3.83 T/MW to 3.64 T/MW resulting saving 
of 21.2 million Kcal of thermal energy annually.
4) Reduced auxiliary power consumption from 9.39% to 9.02% through process enhancement in power plant.
5) Reduction of specific power consumption from 61.88 Kwh to 59.26 Kwh. i.e. 2.62 Kwh/MT of Pellet (152069241 Kwh 
yearly) by enhancement of process parameters to get higher production of 25,65,800 MT.
6) Reduction of specific gas consumption from 180.33 m3/MT to 172.90 m3/MT by enhancement of process parameters 
to save 443632102 m3 of producer gas.

ANNEXURE-06 TO BOARD’S REPORT
CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT
The Board of Directors of the Company pays utmost 
importance on the broad principles of Corporate 
Governance which is modus operandi of governing 
corporate entity which includes a set of systems, 
procedures and practices which ensure that the Company 
is maintaining a valuable relationship and trust with 
all stakeholders. The Company is complying with the 
disclosure norms pursuant to relevant provisions of 
Securities and Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (SEBI 
(LODR) Regulations, 2015).
1. COMPANY’S PHILOSOPHY ON CORPORATE 
GOVERNANCE:
 Your Company prides itself on being a responsible 
corporate citizen, which is committed to running its 
business in the best possible manner while being 
completely transparent, complying with all relevant 
rules & regulations and contributing to society at large. 
The Company believes that maintenance of Code 
of Corporate Governance is essential for economic 
growth of the Company and protecting the interest of 
all the Stakeholders. Therefore, the Company is trying 
its best to follow the Code of Corporate Governance.
 GPIL’s Corporate Governance philosophy  
encompasses not only regulatory and legal 
requirements, such as the terms of listing regulations 
with stock exchanges, but also several voluntary 
practices aimed at a high level of business ethics, 
effective supervision and enhancement of value for 
all stakeholders. The Corporate Governance is based 
on the principal of truth, transparency, accountability, 
equity and responsibility in all our dealings with our 
employees, shareholders, customers, suppliers, 
government, lenders and community at large.
2. CODE OF CONDUCT:
 The Company has in place a comprehensive Code of 
Conduct and Our Code (the “Codes”) applicable to the Directors and employees. The Codes give guidance 
and reflect the core values of the Company viz. 
Ownership Mindset, Respect, Integrity, One Team and 
Excellence.
 The Codes are available on the website of the 
Company. The Codes have been circulated to the 
Directors and Senior Management Personnel and its 
compliance is affirmed by them annually.
3. BOARD OF DIRECTORS:
 a) Composition and category of Directors:
 The Board of Directors has a combination of 
Executive and Non-Executive Directors. The 
Board comprises of Five Executive Directors 
including Chairman-cum-Managing Director and 
Seven Non-Executive Directors. There are Six 
Independent Directors on the Board two of whom 
are Women Directors. The Chairman of the Board 
is Managing Director and is a Non-Independent 
Promoter Director and half of the Board 
comprises of Independent Directors including 
2 Women Directors. Except the Independent 
Directors and Chairman-cum-Managing Director , 
all other Directors are liable to retire by rotation 
as per the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013. 
Accordingly, the composition of the Board is in 
conformity with SEBI (LODR) Regulations, 2015 
and the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013.

including their category, shareholding in the
